# Visuddhimagga

`src:visuddhimagga` · `skeleton` · confidence high

**Alternate titles:** Vism, The Path of Purification
**Original title:** Visuddhimaggo
**Language:** Pali
**Family:** ascetic
**Lineages:** [Theravāda](../lineages/theravada.md), [Mahāvihāra fraternity (Mahāvihāra-vāsins)](../lineages/mahavihara.md)
**Genre:** manual
**Authors:** 
  - [Buddhaghosa](../teachers/buddhaghosa.md) — role: author; attribution: accepted
**Attribution:** tradition: composed by Buddhaghosa at the Mahāvihāra at the request of the elder Saṅghapāla, as a test before he was given the Sinhala commentaries to translate; the gods hid the book twice and he rewrote it identically (Cūḷavaṃsa account); scholarly: Buddhaghosa, early–mid 5th c. CE; confidence: high
**Dates:** Tradition's account: reign of King Mahānāma of Anurādhapura (early 5th c.); Scholarly account: c. 410–450 CE; (confidence moderate)
**Structure:** 23 chapters: I Sīla, II Dhutaṅga (virtue); III Kammaṭṭhānaggahaṇa, IV Pathavīkasiṇa, V Sesakasiṇa, VI Asubha, VII Cha-anussati, VIII Anussati-kammaṭṭhāna, IX Brahmavihāra, X Āruppa, XI Samādhi, XII Iddhividha, XIII Abhiññā (concentration); XIV Khandha, XV Āyatana-dhātu, XVI Indriya-sacca, XVII Paññābhūmi, XVIII Diṭṭhivisuddhi, XIX Kaṅkhāvitaraṇavisuddhi, XX Maggāmaggañāṇadassanavisuddhi, XXI Paṭipadāñāṇadassanavisuddhi, XXII Ñāṇadassanavisuddhi, XXIII Paññābhāvanānisaṃsa (wisdom)
**Availability:** digitized-original

Buddhaghosa's 'Path of Purification': a complete manual of Theravāda doctrine and practice organized under virtue (sīla), concentration (samādhi) and wisdom (paññā) and the seven purifications, with the forty meditation subjects, the supernormal powers, the Abhidhamma analysis of aggregates, bases, faculties, truths and dependent origination, and the stages of insight up to the four paths.
**Editions / translations:** 
  - kind: original; name: ed. C. A. F. Rhys Davids, PTS 1920–21 (GRETIL e-text in sources_raw); licence: CC BY-SA 4.0 (e-text)
  - kind: original; name: ed. H. Kosambi, Harvard Oriental Series 41, 1950
  - kind: translation; name: Pe Maung Tin, The Path of Purity, PTS 1923–31
  - kind: translation; name: Bhikkhu Ñāṇamoli, The Path of Purification, Colombo 1956
**Commentaries on this text:** [Paramatthamañjūsā](paramatthamanjusa.md)

## Teachings (266: skeleton 83, text-verified 183)

### 1 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-1"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Asked by a deity how the inner and outer tangle of craving is disentangled, the Buddha answered: a wise man established in virtue, developing consciousness (concentration) and understanding, ardent and prudent, disentangles this tangle; the Visuddhimagga expounds that verse as virtue, concentration and understanding.

> sīle patiṭṭhāya naro sapañño, cittaṃ paññañ ca bhāvayaṃ, ātāpī nipako bhikkhu, so imaṃ vijaṭaye jaṭan ti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: practice, karma-liberation_

terms: [sīla](../terms/sila.md), [samādhi](../terms/samadhi.md), [paññā](../terms/panna.md) · concepts: [Virtue, concentration, understanding as the frame of the Visuddhimagga](../concepts/three-trainings-vism.md)

### 1 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-1-2"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

'Purification' is to be understood as nibbāna, which is devoid of all stains and utterly pure; the path of purification is the means of reaching it.

> tattha visuddhī ti sabbamalavirahitaṃ, accantaparisuddhaṃ nibbānaṃ veditabbaṃ. tassā visuddhiyā maggo ti visuddhi-maggo.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: analytic · path: general · stage: all · types: ultimate, karma-liberation_

terms: [visuddhi](../terms/visuddhi.md), [nibbāna](../terms/nibbana.md) · concepts: [Nibbāna in the Abhidhamma and commentaries](../concepts/nibbana-abhidhamma.md)

### 1 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-1-3"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

What is virtue? The states beginning with volition present in one who abstains from killing and so on or who fulfils the practice of duties; as the Paṭisambhidāmagga says, volition is virtue, concomitants are virtue, restraint is virtue, non-transgression is virtue.

> kiṃ sīlan ti? cetanā sīlaṃ, cetasikaṃ sīlaṃ, saṃvaro sīlaṃ, avītikkamo sīlan ti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: action · stage: all · types: ethics_

terms: [sīla](../terms/sila.md), [cetana](../terms/cetana.md) · practices: [The fourfold purity of virtue (catupārisuddhi-sīla)](../practices/catuparisuddhi-sila.md)

### 1 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-1-4"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Virtue is fourfold purity: restraint by the Pātimokkha, restraint of the sense faculties, purification of livelihood, and virtue concerning the requisites (using robes, food, lodging and medicine only after reflection on their purpose).

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: action · stage: all (bhikkhu) · types: ethics_

terms: [pātimokkha](../terms/patimokkha.md), [indriyasaṃvara](../terms/indriya-samvara.md) · practices: [The fourfold purity of virtue (catupārisuddhi-sīla)](../practices/catuparisuddhi-sila.md)

### 1 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-1-5"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Wrong livelihood for a monk includes scheming (kuhanā), talking (lapanā), hinting (nemittikatā), belittling (nippesikatā) and pursuing gain with gain — ways of obtaining requisites by deceit or pressure.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: action · stage: all · types: ethics_

terms: [ājīva](../terms/ajiva.md) · practices: [The fourfold purity of virtue (catupārisuddhi-sīla)](../practices/catuparisuddhi-sila.md)

### 2 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-2"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

The Blessed One allowed thirteen ascetic practices to clansmen who have given up worldly things and are without regard for body and life: refuse-rag wearing, triple-robe wearing, alms-food eating, house-to-house seeking, one-sessioner, bowl-food eating, later-food refusing, forest dwelling, tree-root dwelling, open-air dwelling, charnel-ground dwelling, any-bed-user and sitter's practice (never lying down).

> terasa dhutaṅgāni anuññātāni, seyyathīdaṃ: paṃsukūlikaṅgaṃ, tecīvarikaṅgaṃ, piṇḍapātikaṅgaṃ, sāpadānacārikaṅgaṃ, ekāsanikaṅgaṃ, pattapiṇḍikaṅgaṃ, khalupacchābhattikaṅgaṃ, āraññikaṅgaṃ, rukkhamūlikaṅgaṃ, abbhokāsikaṅgaṃ, sosānikaṅgaṃ, yathāsanthatikaṅgaṃ, nesajjikaṅgan ti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: action · stage: intermediate · types: practice, ethics_

terms: [dhutaṅga](../terms/dhutanga.md) · practices: [The thirteen ascetic practices (dhutaṅga)](../practices/dhutanga.md)

### 2 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-2-2"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

The ascetic practices suit one of greedy and one of deluded temperament: they are a painful way and an abiding in effacement; greed subsides through the painful way, and delusion is abandoned by the diligent through effacement.

> kassa dhutaṅgasevanā sappāyā ti rāgacaritassa c' eva mohacaritassa ca. kasmā? dhutaṅgasevanā hi dukkhāpaṭipadā c' eva sallekhavihāro ca.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: all · types: practice_

concepts: [The six temperaments (carita)](../concepts/six-temperaments.md) · practices: [The thirteen ascetic practices (dhutaṅga)](../practices/dhutanga.md)

### 3 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Concentration is wholesome one-pointedness of mind; it concentrates in the sense of placing consciousness and its concomitants evenly and rightly on a single object.

> kusalacittekaggatā samādhi. ken' aṭṭhena samādhī ti? samādhānaṭṭhena samādhi. kim idaṃ samādhānaṃ nāma? ekārammaṇe cittacetasikānaṃ samaṃ sammā ca ādhānaṃ ṭhapanan ti

_level: ultimate · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

terms: [samādhi](../terms/samadhi.md), [ekaggatā](../terms/ekaggata.md) · concepts: [The three kinds of concentration](../concepts/three-samadhis.md)

_Superseded by [3.p84/2](visuddhimagga.md#tea-visuddhimagga-3-p84-2)_

### 3 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-10"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence moderate

Subjects suit temperaments: for the greedy, the ten foulnesses and mindfulness of the body; for the hating, the four divine abidings and the four colour kasiṇas; for the deluded and the speculative, mindfulness of breathing; for the faithful, the first six recollections; for the intelligent, mindfulness of death, the recollection of peace, the defining of the elements and the perception of repulsiveness in nutriment; the remaining kasiṇas and the formless states suit all.

> rāgacaritassa tāva:- ettha dasa asubhā kāyagatāsatī ti ekādasa kammaṭṭhānāni anukulāni. dosacaritassa cattāro brahmavihārā cattāri vaṇṇakasiṇānī ti aṭṭha.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: all · types: practice_

concepts: [The six temperaments (carita)](../concepts/six-temperaments.md), [The forty meditation subjects (kammaṭṭhāna)](../concepts/forty-kammatthanas.md)

_Superseded by [3.p114/3](visuddhimagga.md#tea-visuddhimagga-3-p114-3)_

### 3 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-11"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

A monastery unfavourable to concentration has one of eighteen faults, such as being large, new, dilapidated, near a road, having a pond, leaves, flowers or fruit, being sought after, near a city, timber or fields, having incompatible persons, near a port, a border or a frontier, being unsuitable, and lacking good friends.

> tatr' ime aṭṭhārasa dosā:- mahattaṃ, navattaṃ, jiṇṇattaṃ, panthanissitattaṃ, soṇḍī, paṇṇaṃ, pupphaṃ, phalaṃ, patthanīyatā, nagarasannissitatā, dārusannissitatā, khettasannissitatā, visabhāgānaṃ puggalānaṃ atthitā, paṭṭanasannissitatā, paccantasannissitatā, rajjasīmasannissitatā, asappāyatā, kalyāṇamittānaṃ alābho ti

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: all · types: practice_

obstacles: [The eighteen faults of a monastery](../obstacles/eighteen-monastery-faults.md)

### 3 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-2"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Concentration is twofold as access and absorption: the unification reached through the six recollections, mindfulness of death, recollection of peace, the perception of repulsiveness in nutriment and the defining of the elements, and the unification preceding absorption, is access concentration; the unification immediately following the preliminary work is absorption.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: practice_

terms: [upacāra-samādhi](../terms/upacara-samadhi.md), [appanā-samādhi](../terms/appana-samadhi.md) · concepts: [The three kinds of concentration](../concepts/three-samadhis.md)

_Superseded by [3.p85/3](visuddhimagga.md#tea-visuddhimagga-3-p85-3)_

### 3 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-3"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

One who would develop concentration must first sever whichever of the ten impediments he has: a dwelling, a family, gain, a following, building work, travel, kin, affliction, books, and supernormal powers.

> āvāso ca kulaṃ lābho, gaṇo kammañ ca pañcamaṃ; addhānaṃ, ñāti, ābādho, gantho, iddhī ti te dasā ti ime dasa paḷibodhā nāma.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: all · types: practice_

terms: [paḷibodha](../terms/palibodha.md) · obstacles: [The ten impediments (paḷibodha)](../obstacles/ten-palibodhas.md)

_Superseded by [3.p90](visuddhimagga.md#tea-visuddhimagga-3-p90)_

### 3 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-4"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

The worldling's supernormal power is hard to keep up, like an infant lying on its back or young corn, and is broken by a trifle; it is an impediment for insight but not for concentration, since it is reached through concentration — so one who seeks insight should sever it.

> iddhī ti pothujjanikā iddhi. sā hi uttānaseyyakadārako viya, taruṇasassaṃ viya ca dupparihārā hoti, appamattaken' eva bhijjati. sā pana vipassanāya paḷibodho hoti, na samādhissa

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, practice_

terms: [iddhi](../terms/iddhi.md) · concepts: [The six direct knowledges](../concepts/six-abhinnas.md) · obstacles: [The ten impediments (paḷibodha)](../obstacles/ten-palibodhas.md)

_Superseded by [3.p97/2](visuddhimagga.md#tea-visuddhimagga-3-p97-2)_

### 3 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-5"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

The meditation subject is to be taken from a good friend who gives it: one who is lovable, respected, esteemed, a counsellor, patient with speech, who can give deep talk and does not urge to what is useless; the Buddha is the best such friend, for relying on him beings subject to birth are freed from birth.

> piyo garu bhāvanīyo vattā ca vacanakkhamo, gambhīrañ ca kathaṅkattā, no ca ṭhāne niyojaye ti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: beginner · types: teacher-transmission_

terms: [kalyāṇamitta](../terms/kalyanamitta.md), [kammaṭṭhāna](../terms/kammatthana.md) · concepts: [Good friendship](../concepts/kalyanamittata.md)

_Superseded by [3.p98/2](visuddhimagga.md#tea-visuddhimagga-3-p98-2)_

### 3 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-6"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

There are six temperaments — greedy, hating, deluded, faithful, intelligent and speculative; some posit fourteen by combining them, but combinations would be endless, so in brief there are six; 'temperament', 'nature' and 'predominance' mean the same.

> cariyā ti cha cariyā: rāgacariyā, dosacariyā, mohacariyā, saddhācariyā, buddhicariyā, vitakkacariyā ti. keci pana rāgādīnaṃ saṃsaggasannipātavasena aparāpi catasso, tathā saddhādīnan ti imāhi aṭṭhahi saddhiṃ cuddasa icchanti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: consciousness-mind, practice_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md) · concepts: [The six temperaments (carita)](../concepts/six-temperaments.md) · disputes: [Where the Visuddhimagga departs from the Vimuttimagga (e.g. on the number of temperaments), which account holds?](../debates/vimuttimagga-visuddhimagga.md)

_Superseded by [3.p101/2](visuddhimagga.md#tea-visuddhimagga-3-p101-2)_

### 3 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-7"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Because faith is strong in the wholesome moments of one of greedy temperament, faith is to the wholesome side what greed is to the unwholesome: as greed seeks sense objects, faith seeks virtue and other special qualities.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation, devotion · stage: beginner · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [saddhā](../terms/saddha.md) · concepts: [The six temperaments (carita)](../concepts/six-temperaments.md)

_Superseded by [3.p102](visuddhimagga.md#tea-visuddhimagga-3-p102)_

### 3 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-8"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

The forty meditation subjects: ten kasiṇas (earth, water, fire, air, blue, yellow, red, white, light, limited space), ten foulnesses (bloated, livid, festering, cut-up, gnawed, scattered, hacked-and-scattered, bleeding, worm-infested, skeleton), ten recollections (Buddha, Dhamma, Saṅgha, virtue, generosity, deities, death, mindfulness of the body, mindfulness of breathing, peace), four divine abidings, four formless states, one perception and one defining.

> dasa kasiṇā, dasa asubhā, dasa anussatiyo, cattāro brahmavihārā, cattāro āruppā, ekā saññā, ekaṃ vavatthānan ti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: all · types: practice_

terms: [kammaṭṭhāna](../terms/kammatthana.md), [kasiṇa](../terms/kasina.md), [asubha](../terms/asubha.md), [anussati](../terms/anussati.md), [brahmavihāra](../terms/brahmavihara.md) · concepts: [The forty meditation subjects (kammaṭṭhāna)](../concepts/forty-kammatthanas.md)

_Superseded by [3.p110/2](visuddhimagga.md#tea-visuddhimagga-3-p110-2)_

### 3 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-9"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Ten subjects bring only access — the eight recollections other than mindfulness of the body and of breathing, the perception of repulsiveness in nutriment and the defining of the elements; the rest bring absorption: the ten kasiṇas and mindfulness of breathing all four jhānas, the ten foulnesses and body-mindfulness the first jhāna, the first three divine abidings three jhānas, the fourth divine abiding and the four formless states the fourth.

> ṭhapetvā kāyagatāsatiñ ca ānāpānassatiñ ca avasesā aṭṭha anussatiyo, āhāre paṭikūlasaññā, catudhātuvavatthānan ti imān' eva h' ettha dasa kammaṭṭhānāni upacārāvahāni; sesāni appanāvahānī ti

_level: ultimate · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: practice_

concepts: [The forty meditation subjects (kammaṭṭhāna)](../concepts/forty-kammatthanas.md), [The three kinds of concentration](../concepts/three-samadhis.md)

_Superseded by [3.p111/2](visuddhimagga.md#tea-visuddhimagga-3-p111-2)_

### 3.p100 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p100"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Manner of approaching the teacher. Ask for the teacher's dwelling and go straight there. If he is younger, do not accept the taking of one's bowl and robe; if elder, salute and stand; put down the bowl and robe only when told; drink water only when told; wash the feet only when told, and then out of his sight, since water he brought would make it improper; take the oil-tube in both hands if he brings it, and anoint first the head then shoulders, not the feet first; on the day of arrival, do not say "teach me the subject"; from the second day serve him, asking the regular attendant or, if refused, when there is a chance; bring three tooth-sticks, cold and warm water for mouth and bathing, given as the teacher habitually uses them.

> āgato1 ti vippaṭisāraṃ uppādeyyuṃ, yena tato va paṭinivat-  
teya, tasmā ācariyassa vasanaṭṭhānaṃ pucchitvā ujukaṃ  
tatth'; eva gantabbaṃ| sace ācariyo daharataro hoti, patta-  
cīvarapaṭiggahaṇādīni na sāditabbāni| sace vuḍḍhataro2  
hoti, gantvā ācariyaṃ vanditvā ṭhātabbaṃ: nikkhip'; āvuso  
pattacīvaran ti vuttena nikkhipitabbaṃ: pānīyaṃ pi vā ti  
vuttena, sace icchati, pātabbaṃ: pāde dhovā ti3 vuttena na  
tāva pādā dhovitabbā| sace hi ācariyena ābhataṃ udakaṃ4  
bhaveyya, na sāruppaṃ siyā| dhov', āvuso,5 na mayā  
ābhataṃ, aññehi ābhatan ti vuttena pana, yattha ācariyo  
na passati, evarūpe paṭicchanne vā okāse abbhokāse6  
vihārassā pi vā ekamante nisīditvā pādā dhovitabbā| sace  
ācariyo telanāḷiṃ āharati, uṭṭhahitvā ubhohi hatthehi  
sakkaccaṃ gahetabbā| sace hi na gaṇheyya: ayaṃ  
bhikkhu ito eva7 paṭṭhāya sambhogaṃ kopetī ti ācariyassa  
aññathattaṃ bhaveyya; gahetvā pana na ādito va pādā  
makkhetabbā| sace hi taṃ ācariyassa gattabbhañjanatelaṃ  
bhaveyya, na sāruppaṃ siyā; tasmā pathamaṃ7 sīsaṃ mak-  
khetvā khandhādīni makkhetabbāni| sabbapārihāriyate-  
lam: idaṃ āvuso pāde pi makkhehī ti vuttena pana, thokaṃ  
sīse katvā,8 pāde makkhetvā: imaṃ telanāḷiṃ ṭhapemi, bhan-  
te ti vatvā ācariye gaṇhante dātabbā, āgatadivasato paṭṭhā-  
ya: kammaṭṭhānaṃ me bhante kathetha icc'; evaṃ na vattab-  
baṃ| dutiyadivasato pana paṭṭhāya sace ācariyassa pakati-  
upaṭṭhāko atthi, taṃ yācitvā vattaṃ kātabbaṃ| sace  
yācito pi na deti, okāse laddhe yeva kātabbaṃ| karontena  
ca7 khuddakamajjhimamahantāni tīṇi dantakaṭṭhāni upanā-  
metabbāni, sītaṃ uṇhan ti duvidhaṃ mukhadhovana-uda-  
kañ ca nahānodakañ ca paṭiyādetabbaṃ; tato yaṃ ācariyo  
tīṇi divasāni paribhuñjati tādisam eva niccaṃ upanāmetab-  
baṃ| niyamaṃ akatvā yaṃ vā taṃ vā paribhuñjantassa  
yathā-laddhaṃ upanāmetabbaṃ| kiṃ bahunā? vuttena,  
yan taṃ bhagavatā: antevāsikena bhikkhave ācariyamhi

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice, ethics_

practices: [Good friendship (kalyāṇamittatā), the teacher who gives the subject](../practices/kalyanamittata.md)

### 3.p101 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p101"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The pupil's duties toward the teacher are those laid down in the Khandhaka (rising early, tooth-stick, mouth water, seat, rice-gruel and so on); with these done, salute in the evening and go when told. When asked why he has come, state the reason; if not asked and the teacher accepts service, after ten days or a fortnight, request an opportunity and state it. If asked to come early, come early; if at that hour one has an illness (for instance bile trouble, poor digestion), tell him truthfully and state the hour that suits, since a subject told at an unsuitable hour cannot be attended to.

> sammā vattitabbaṃ| tatrāyaṃ sammāvattanā: kālass'; eva  
uṭṭhāya, upāhanā omuñcitvā, ekaṃsaṃ uttarāsangaṃ karitvā,  
dantakaṭṭhaṃ dātabbaṃ, mukhodakaṃ dātabbaṃ, āsanaṃ pañ-  
ñapetabbaṃ|1 sace yāgu hoti, bhājanaṃ dhovitvā, yāgu upa-  
nāmetabbā ti ādikaṃ khandhake2 sammāvattaṃ paññattaṃ,  
taṃ sabbam pi kātabbaṃ| evaṃ vattasampattiyā garuṃ3  
ārādhayamānena sāyaṃ vanditvā: yāhī ti vissajjitena gantab-  
baṃ| yadā so: kissa āgato4 'sī ti pucchati, tadā āgamanakā-  
raṇaṃ kathetabbaṃ| sace so neva pucchati, vattaṃ pana  
sādiyati, dasāhe5 vā pakkhe vā vītivatte ekadivasaṃ vissaj-  
jitenā pi agantvā okāsaṃ kāretvā āgamanakāraṇaṃ ārocetab-  
baṃ| akāle vā gantvā: kim attham6 āgato 'sī ti puṭṭhena āro-  
cetabbaṃ| sace so: pāto va āgacchā ti vadati, pāto va  
gantabbaṃ| sace pan'; assa tāya velāya7 pittābādhena vā  
kucchi pariḍayhati, aggimandatāya vā bhattaṃ na jīrati,  
añño vā koci rogo bādhati, taṃ yathābhūtaṃ āvikatvā attano  
sappāyavelaṃ ārocetvā tāya velāya8 upasankamitabbaṃ,  
asappāyavelāya9 hi vuccamānam pi kammaṭṭhānaṃ na sakkā  
hoti, manasikātun ti|

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice, ethics, teacher-transmission_

practices: [Good friendship (kalyāṇamittatā), the teacher who gives the subject](../practices/kalyanamittata.md)

### 3.p101 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p101-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

On "suited to one's own temperament (cariya)". There are six temperaments (cariyā): the greedy (rāgacariyā), the hating (dosacariyā), the deluded (mohacariyā), the faithful (saddhācariyā), the intelligent (buddhicariyā) and the speculative (vitakkacariyā). Some add four more by combinations of greed and the others, and four by combinations of the faith group, making fourteen; but mixing greed and the rest with the faith group would give many temperaments, so in brief only six are to be understood.

> idāni attano cariyānukulan ti ettha cariyā ti cha cariyā:  
rāgacariyā, dosacariyā, mohacariyā, saddhācariyā, buddhi-  
cariyā, vitakkacariyā ti| keci pana rāgādīnaṃ saṃsagga-  
sannipātavasena aparāpi catasso, tathā saddhādīnan ti imāhi  
aṭṭhahi saddhiṃ cuddasa icchanti| evaṃ pana bhede vuc-  
camāne rāgādīnaṃ saddhādīhi pi saṃsaggaṃ katvā aneka-  
cariyā honti, tasmā sankhepena chaḷ eva cariyā veditabbā|

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md) · concepts: [The six temperaments (carita)](../concepts/six-temperaments.md)

### 3.p102 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p102"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The six temperaments are the greedy, hating, deluded, faithful, intelligent and speculative person. The commentator gives the affinity of three pairs. In the greedy person faith is strong when wholesome states occur, because faith is a near-kin quality of greed: as greed is sticky and not too rough on the unwholesome side, so is faith on the wholesome; as greed seeks sensual objects, faith seeks the virtues; as greed does not give up what harms, faith does not give up what benefits. So the faithful temperament is akin to the greedy. In the hating person understanding is strong, since it is a near-kin of hate: hate does not cling to an object, nor does understanding; hate looks for faults even where none, understanding looks for real faults; hate turns away from beings, understanding from formations. So the intelligent temperament is akin to the hating. In the deluded person, striving to arouse wholesome states, obstructing thoughts mostly arise, since thinking is close to delusion: delusion is unsettled through confusion, thinking through varied thinking; delusion wavers for lack of depth, thinking through quick imagining. So the speculative temperament is akin to the deluded.

> eva puggalā honti rāgacarito, dosacarito, mohacarito, sad-  
dhācarito, buddhacarito, vitakkacarito ti| tattha yasmā  
rāgacaritassa kusalappavattisamaye saddhā balavatī hoti,  
rāgassa āsannaguṇattā| yathā hi akusalapakkhe rāgo si-  
niddho, nātilūkho, evaṃ kusalapakkhe saddhā| yathā rāgo  
vatthukāme pariyesati, evaṃ saddhā sīlādi guṇe| yathā  
rāgo ahitaṃ na pariccajati, evaṃ saddhā hitaṃ na pariccajati,  
tasmā rāgacaritassa saddhācarito sabhāgo| yasmā pana  
dosacaritassa kusalappavattisamaye paññā balavatī hoti,  
dosassa āsannaguṇattā| yathā hi akusalapakkhe doso nis-  
sineho1 na ārammaṇaṃ allīyati, evaṃ kusalapakkhe paññā|  
yathā ca doso abhūtam pi2 dosam eva pariyesati, evaṃ|  
paññā bhūtaṃ dosam eva pariyesati|3 yathā doso sattapari-  
vajjanākārena pavattati, evaṃ paññā sankhāraparivajja-  
nākārena| tasmā dosacaritassa buddhicarito sabhāgo| yas-  
mā pana mohacaritassa anuppannānaṃ kusalānaṃ dham-  
mānaṃ uppādāya vāyāmamānassa yebhuyyena antarāya-  
karā vitakkā uppajjanti, mohassa āsannalakkhaṇattā| ya-  
thā hi moho parivyākulatāya anavaṭṭhito, evaṃ vitakko  
nānappakāravitakkanatāya| yathā ca moho apariyogāha-  
ṇatāya cañcalo, tathā vitakko lahuparikappanatāya| tasmā  
mohacaritassa vitakkacarito sabhāgo ti|

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md) · concepts: [The six temperaments (carita)](../concepts/six-temperaments.md)

### 3.p102 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p102-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Others count three more temperaments, by craving, conceit and view. The text replies that craving is just greed and conceit is associated with it, so both fall within the greedy temperament; and since view has delusion as its ground, the view-temperament follows the deluded. It then poses the questions: what are the causes of these temperaments; how is it known that this person is greedy or of another type; what suits a person of each temperament?

> apare taṇhā-māna-diṭṭhivasena aparā pi tisso cariyā va-  
danti| tattha taṇhārāgo yeva, māno ca taṃ sampayutto ti  
tadubhayaṃ rāgacariyaṃ nātivattati| mohanidānattā ca  
diṭṭhiyā diṭṭhicariyā mohacariyam eva anupatati|  
     tā pan'; etā cariyā kiṃ nidānā? kathañ ca jānitabbaṃ3:  
ayaṃ puggalo rāgacarito, ayaṃ puggalo4 dosādisu aññatara-  
carito ti? kiṃ caritassa puggalassa? kiṃ sappāyan ti?

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p102 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p102-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

On the causes. Some say the first three temperaments have as their cause past conduct or an imbalance of the elements. A person of much former pleasing conduct and good action is greedy, or has fallen from heaven and been reborn here; one who much practised cutting, killing, binding and enmity is hating, or has fallen from hell or from the nāga-realm; one who much drank spirits and lacked learning and inquiry is deluded, or has fallen from the animal realm.

> tatra purimā tāva tisso cariyā pubbāciṇṇanidānā dhātudo-  
sanidānā cā ti ekacce vadanti| pubbe kira iṭṭhappayoga-  
subhakammabahulo rāgacarito hoti; saggā vā cavitvā idhū-  
papanno| pubbe chedanavadhabandhanaverakammabahu-  
lo5 dosacarito hoti; nirayanāgayonīhi vā cavitvā idhūpa-

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p103 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p103"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Others say by the elements and humours: from a preponderance of the earth and water elements a person is deluded; of the other two, hating; when all are balanced, greedy. Among the humours, one with more phlegm is greedy, with more wind deluded, or with more phlegm deluded, with more wind greedy; so the cause is the elements and the humours.

> dvinnaṃ pana dhātūnaṃ ussannattā puggalo mohacarito  
hoti: pathavīdhātuyā ca āpodhātuyā ca| itarāsaṃ dvinnaṃ  
ussannattā dosacarito| sabbāsaṃ samattā pana rāgacarito ti|  
dosesu ca semhādhiko rāgacarito hoti, vātādhiko mohacarito,  
semhādhiko vā mohacarito, vātādhiko vā rāgacarito ti evaṃ  
dhātudosanidānā ti vadanti|

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: body-layers, consciousness-mind_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p103 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p103-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The commentator's verdict. Because not everyone who did much good conduct or fell from heaven is greedy, nor the others deluded, and because there is no fixed preponderance of the elements as stated, and because the humour-account names only two (greed and delusion) and contradicts itself, and because no cause is given for the faithful and so on, all this is left undecided; the reasoning of the Commentary teachers is given instead. Beings differ by preponderance (ussada) of greed, hate, delusion or of non-greed, non-hate, non-delusion, fixed by the earlier cause. When at the moment of accumulating kamma greed is strong and non-greed weak, and non-hate and non-delusion strong, hate and delusion weak, the weak non-greed cannot overcome greed but the strong ones do overcome hate and delusion; so the person is born greedy, easy-natured, not angry, and wise, with diamond-like knowledge; other combinations give other results, e.g. greedy and hating but wise like the elder Dattābhaya.

> tattha yasmā pubbe iṭṭhappayogasubhakammabahulā1 pi  
saggā cavitvā idhūpapannā2 pi ca, na sabbe rāgacaritā yeva  
honti,3 itare vā dosamohacaritā, evaṃ dhātūnañ ca yathāvut-  
ten'; eva nayena ussadaniyamo nāma n'; atthi| dosaniyame ca  
rāgamohadvayam eva vuttaṃ; tam pi ca pubbāparavirud-  
dham eva| saddhācariyādisu ca ekissā pi nidānaṃ na vuttam  
eva, tasmā sabbam etaṃ aparicchinnavacanaṃ| ayaṃ  
pan'; ettha aṭṭhakathācariyānaṃ matānusārena vinicchayo|  
vuttaṃ h'; etaṃ ussadakittane:- ime sattā pubbahetuniyā-  
mena4 lobhussadā dosussadā mohussadā alobhussadā adosussadā  
amohussadā ca honti| yassa hi kammāyūhanakkhaṇe lobho  
balavā hoti, alobho mando, adosāmohā balavanto, dosamohā  
mandā, tassa mando alobho lobhaṃ pariyādātuṃ na sakkoti,  
adosāmohā pana balavanto dosamohe pariyādātuṃ sakkonti|  
tasmā so, tena kammena dinnapaṭisandhivasena nibbatto,  
luddho hoti sukhasīlo akodhano paññavā vajirūpamañāṇo|  
yassa pana kammāyūhanakkhaṇe lobhadosā balavanto honti  
alobhādosā mandā, amoho ca balavā, moho mando, so purimana-  
yen'; eva luddho ceva hoti duṭṭho ca, paññavā pana hoti vajirū-  
pamañāṇo dattābhayatthero viya|

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p104 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p104"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The scheme continues through the other combinations (for instance, one strong in non-greed, hate and delusion is free of greed and little defiled but hating and dull; one strong in all three of non-greed, non-hate and non-delusion is not greedy, not hating, and wise, like the elder Mahāsaṅgharakkhita). In this, the "greedy" is the greedy temperament; the hating and dull are the hating and deluded temperaments; the wise is the intelligent temperament; those neither greedy nor hating, of a pleasant nature, are the faithful. The one born of kamma with a following of faith is the faithful; with a following of sensual thoughts, the speculative; with a following mixed of greed and the rest, the mixed. So kamma with the retinue of one of greed and so on that gives rebirth-linking is to be understood as the cause of the temperaments.

> yassa pana kammāyūhanakkhaṇe alobhadosamohā balavanto  
honti, itare mandā, so purimanayen'; eva aluddho appakileso  
hoti, dibbārammaṇam pi disvā niccalo, duṭṭho pana hoti, dandha-  
pañño ca| yassa kammāyūhanakkhaṇe alobhādosamohā ba-  
lavanto honti, itare mandā, so purimanayen'; eva aluddho c'; eva  
hoti aduṭṭho sīlako ca, dandho pana hoti| tathā yassa kammā-  
yūhanakkhaṇe alobhadosāmohā balavanto honti, itare mandā,  
so purimanayen'; eva aluddho c'; eva hoti, paññavā ca, duṭṭho  
pana hoti, kodhano| yassa pana 1kammāyūhanakkhaṇe tayo  
pi alobhādosāmohā2 balavanto honti, lobhādayo mandā, so  
purimanayen'; eva, mahāsangharakkhitatthero viya, aluddho  
aduṭṭho paññavā ca hotī ti|  
     ettha ca yo luddho ti vutto, ayaṃ rāgacarito; duṭṭhadan-  
dhā dosamohacaritā; paññavā buddhicarito, aluddha-  
aduṭṭhā3 pasannapakatitāya saddhācaritā| yathā vā amoha-  
parivārena kammunā4 nibbatto buddhicarito, evaṃ balava-  
saddhāparivārena kammunā nibbatto saddhācarito, kāma-  
vitakkādiparivārena kammunā nibbatto vitakkacarito| lo-  
bhādinā vomissa-parivārena kammunā nibbatto vomissa-  
carito ti| evaṃ lobhādisu aññataraññataraparivāraṃ pa-  
ṭisandhijanakaṃ kammaṃ cariyānaṃ nidānan ti veditabbaṃ|

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md) · concepts: [The six temperaments (carita)](../concepts/six-temperaments.md)

### 3.p104 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p104-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

How temperaments are known. A verse gives five means: from posture (iriyāpatha), from work (kicca), from eating (bhojana), from seeing (dassana) and so on, and from the arising of states (dhammappavatti). By posture: the greedy person, walking naturally, goes gracefully, puts the foot down slowly and evenly, lifts it evenly, and his footprint is "raised" (ukkuṭika). The hating person walks as if digging with the toe-tips, puts the foot down abruptly, lifts it abruptly, and his footprint is dragged along (anukaḍḍhita). The deluded person walks in a bewildered gait, puts the foot down as if startled, lifts it as if startled, and (continued on p. 105).

> yaṃ pana vuttaṃ: kathañ ca jānitabbaṃ: ayaṃ puggalo  
rāgacarito ti ādi,5 tatrāyaṃ nayo:-  
          iriyāpathato kiccā bhojanā dassanādito  
          dhammappavattito c'; eva cariyāyo vibhāvaye ti|  
     tattha6 iriyāpathato ti rāgacarito hi pakatigamanena  
gacchanto cāturiyena gacchati, saṇikaṃ pādaṃ nikkhipati,  
samaṃ nikkhipati, samaṃ uddharati, ukkuṭikañ c'; assa padaṃ  
hoti| dosacarito pādaggehi khaṇanto viya gacchati, sahasā  
pādaṃ nikkhipati, sahasā uddharati, anukaḍḍhitañ7 c'; assa  
padaṃ hoti| mohacarito parivyākulāya gatiyā gacchati,  
chambhito viya pādaṃ nikkhipati, chambhito viya uddharati,

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: consciousness-mind, practice_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p105 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p105"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Posture (iriyāpatha) continued. The deluded person's footprint is abruptly pressed. A verse from the Māgandiya-sutta occasion says: for one with passion the footprint is "raised"; for the hating it is dragged along; for the deluded it is abruptly pressed; for one whose veil is drawn back it is such (as that of a Buddha). Even the manner of standing (māna) is pleasing and sweet-looking in the greedy, rigid in the hating, confused in the deluded; so it is in sitting. In lying down: the greedy, unhurried, lays out the bed evenly, lies down slowly with limbs gathered in a pleasing way, and when woken rises quickly and answers slowly as if in doubt. The hating, hurried, lays out the bed anyhow, throws himself down with a frown, and when woken rises quickly and answers as if angry. The deluded lays out the bed badly, sprawls, mostly face down, and when woken makes a grunt and rises sluggishly. Those of the faithful temperament and the rest, being akin to the greedy and the rest, have the same postures.

> sahasānupīḷitañ c'; assa padaṃ hoti| vuttam pi c'; etaṃ  
māgandiyasuttuppattiyaṃ1:  
          rattassa hi ukkuṭikaṃ padaṃ bhave,  
          duṭṭhassa hoti anukaḍḍhitaṃ padaṃ,  
          mūḷhassa padaṃ sahasānupīḷitaṃ,  
          vivaṭṭacchadass'2 idam īdisaṃ padan ti|  
     mānam pi rāgacaritassa pasādikaṃ hoti madhurākāraṃ,  
dosacaritassa thaddhākāraṃ, mohacaritassa ākulākāraṃ|  
nisajjāya pi es'; eva nayo| rāgacarito ca ataramāno samaṃ  
seyyaṃ paññāpetvā saṇikaṃ nipajjitvā angapaccangāni  
samodhāya pāsādikena ākārena sayati, vuṭṭhāpiyamāno ca  
sīghaṃ vuṭṭhāya3 sankito viya saṇikaṃ paṭivacanaṃ deti|  
dosacarito taramāno yathā vā4 tathā vā seyyaṃ paññāpetvā  
pakkhittakāyo bhākuṭikaṃ5 katvā sayati, vuṭṭhāpiyamāno  
ca sīghaṃ vuṭṭhāya kupito viya paṭivacanaṃ deti| moha-  
carito dussaṇṭhānaṃ seyyaṃ paññāpetvā vikkhittakāyo ba-  
hulaṃ adhomukho sayati, vuṭṭhāpiyamāno ca hunkāraṃ ka-  
ronto dandhaṃ vuṭṭhāti| saddhācaritādayo pana yasmā  
rāgacaritādīnaṃ sabhāgā, tasmā tesam pi tādiso va iriyā-  
patho hotī ti| evaṃ tāva iriyāpathato cariyāyo vibhāvaye|

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p105 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p105-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Work (kicca), for instance sweeping. The greedy person takes a good broom and sweeps unhurried, not scattering the sand, spreading it like a carpet of flowers, clean and even. The hating person takes a hard grip on the broom, sweeps in a hurry, throwing sand up on both sides with a harsh noise, leaving it unclean and uneven. The deluded person holds the broom loosely and sweeps turning it round and stirring it up, unclean and uneven. As in sweeping, so in washing and dyeing robes and all work.

> kiccā ti sammajjanādisu ca kiccesu rāgacarito sādhukaṃ  
sammajjaniṃ gahetvā ataramāno vālikaṃ avippakiranto  
sindhuvārakusumasantharam iva santharanto suddhaṃ  
samaṃ sammajjati| dosacarito gālhaṃ sammajjaniṃ  
gahetvā taramānarūpo ubhato vālikaṃ ussārento6  
kharena saddena asuddhaṃ visaṃam sammajjati| moha-  
carito sithilaṃ sammajjaniṃ gahetvā samparivattakaṃ  
āḷolayamāno asuddhaṃ visamaṃ sammajjati| yathā ca  
sammajjane, evaṃ cīvaradhovanarajanādisu pi sabbakiccesu|

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p106 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p106"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Work concluded: the greedy is skilful, gentle, even and careful in his work; the hating firm, rigid and uneven; the deluded unskilful, confused, uneven and unfinished. Wearing the robe: the greedy's is neither too tight nor too loose, pleasing and neat; the hating's too tight and untidy; the deluded's loose and confused. The faithful and the rest are to be known by following the same, being akin.

> kāre1 mohacarito| cīvaradhāranam pi ca rāgacaritassa nāti-  
gāḷhaṃ2 nātisithilaṃ hoti pāsādikaṃ parimaṇḍalaṃ; dosacari-  
tassa atigāḷhaṃ aparimaṇḍalaṃ; mohacaritassa sithilaṃ pari-  
vyākulaṃ| saddhācaritādayo tesaṃ yevānusārena vedi-  
tabbā, taṃ sabhāgattā ti| evaṃ kiccato cariyāyo vibhā-  
vaye|

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p106 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p106-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Eating (bhojana). The greedy person likes rich and sweet food; eating, he makes a moderate round mouthful, tastes the various flavours unhurried, and is glad if he gets something nice. The hating person likes rough and sour food; he makes a mouthful that fills the mouth, tastes nothing much, eats in a hurry, and is displeased if he gets something unpleasant. The deluded has no fixed taste; eating, he makes an untidy small mouthful, drops food in the bowl, smears his mouth, and eats with a distracted mind thinking of this and that. The faithful and the rest are to be known by following the same, being akin.

> bhojanā ti rāgacarito siniddhamadhurabhojanappiyo3  
hoti; bhuñjamāno ca nātimahantaṃ parimaṇḍalaṃ alopaṃ  
katvā nānārasapatisaṃvedī-4ataramāno bhuñjati, kiñcideva  
ca sāduṃ labhitvā somanassaṃ āpajjati; dosacarito lūkha-  
ambilabhojanaṃ piyo3 hoti; bhuñjamāno ca mukhapūrakaṃ  
ālopaṃ katvā arasapaṭisaṃvedī taramāno bhuñjati, kincid-  
eva ca asāduṃ labhitvā, domanassaṃ āpajjati| mohacarito  
aniyataruciko hoti, bhuñjamāno ca aparimaṇḍalaṃ parittaṃ  
ālopaṃ5 katvā bhājane chaḍḍento mukhaṃ makkhento vik-  
khittacitto taṃ taṃ vitakkento bhuñjati| saddhācaritādayo  
pi tesaṃ yevānusārena veditabbā, taṃ sabhāgattā ti| evaṃ  
bhojanato cariyāyo vibhāvaye|

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p106 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p106-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Seeing and so on (dassanādi). The greedy person, on seeing even a slightly pleasing sight, looks long as if amazed, clings even to a small good quality, does not take note of a real fault, and departs with reluctance and looking back. The hating person, on seeing even a slightly unpleasing sight, looks as if tired and does not look long, is offended by a small fault, does not take note of a real virtue, and departs as if wishing to be rid of it, without looking back. The deluded, on seeing whatever it is, depends on others: he blames when he hears another blame, praises when he hears praise, but himself, from ignorance, is indifferent. The same holds for hearing sounds and the rest; the faithful and the rest follow, being akin.

> dassanādito ti rāgacarito īsakam pi manoramaṃ rūpaṃ  
disvā vimhayajāto viya ciraṃ oloketi, paritte pi guṇe sajjati,  
bhūtam pi dosaṃ na gaṇhāti, pakkamanto pi amuñcitukāmo  
va hutvā sāpekkho pakkamati| dosacarito īsakam pi ama-  
noramaṃ rūpaṃ6 disvā kilantarūpo viya na ciraṃ oloketi,  
paritte pi dose paṭihaññati, bhūtam pi guṇaṃ na gaṇhāti,  
pakkamanto pi muñcitukāmo va hutvā anapekkho pakkam-  
ati| mohacarito yaṃ kiñci rūpaṃ disvā parapaccayiko  
hoti, paraṃ nindantaṃ sutvā nindati, pasaṃsantaṃ sutvā  
pasaṃsati, sayaṃ pana aññāṇupekkhāya upekkhako va  
hoti| esa nayo saddasavanādisu pi| saddhācaritādayo pa-  
na tesaṃ yevānusārena veditabbā, taṃ sabhāgattā ti| evaṃ  
dassanādito cariyāyo vibhāvaye|

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p106 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p106-4"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

States (dhammappavatti). In the greedy person deceit, craftiness, conceit, evil wishes, great wishes, discontent, ostentation, fickleness and the like mostly occur.

> dhammappavattito cevā ti rāgacaritassa ca māyā, sāthey-  
yaṃ, māno, pāpicchatā, mahicchatā, asantuṭṭhitā, singaṃ,  
cāpalyan ti evam ādayo dhammā bahulaṃ pavattanti|

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p107 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p107"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

In the hating person anger, grudge, contempt, domineering, envy, avarice and the like mostly occur. In the deluded: sloth, torpor, restlessness, worry, doubt, holding tight (to views), hard to let go. In the faithful: generosity, wish to see the noble ones, wish to hear the good Dhamma, abundant gladness, uncraftiness, undeceitfulness, confidence in places that inspire it. In the intelligent: being easy to admonish, having good friends, moderation in eating, mindfulness and clear comprehension, devotion to wakefulness, a sense of urgency at what should stir it, and right striving in one so stirred. In the speculative: talkativeness, fondness for company, dislike of undertaking wholesome work, an unsettled mind, "smoking by night, blazing by day", and rushing hither and thither.

> dosacaritassa kodho, upanāho, makkho, paḷāso, issā, mac-  
chariyan ti evam ādayo| mohacaritassa thīnaṃ, middhaṃ,  
uddhaccaṃ kukkuccaṃ, vicikicchā, ādānagāhitā, duppaṭi-  
nissaggitā ti evam ādayo| saddhācaritassa muttacāgatā,  
ariyānaṃ dassanakāmatā, saddhammaṃ sotukāmatā, pā-  
mojjabahulatā, asaṃsaṭhatā,1 amāyāvitā, pasādanīyesu ṭhā-  
nesu pasādo ti evam ādayo| buddhicaritassa sovacassatā,  
kalyāṇamittatā, bhojane mattaññutā, satisampajaññaṃ, jā-  
gariyānuyogo, saṃvejanīyesu ṭhānesu saṃvego, saṃviggassa  
ca2 yoniso padhānan ti3 evam ādayo| vitakkacaritassa  
bhassabahulatā, gaṇārāmatā, kusalānuyoge arati, anavaṭ-  
ṭhitacittatā,4 rattidhūmāyanā,5 divāpajjalanā, hurāhuraṃ  
dhāvanā ti evam ādayo dhammā bahulaṃ pavattantī ti|  
evaṃ dhammappavattito cariyāyo vibhāvaye|

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md), [nīvaraṇa](../terms/nivarana.md) · obstacles: [Sloth and torpor (thīnamiddha)](../obstacles/thina-middha.md), [Restlessness and remorse (uddhaccakukkucca)](../obstacles/uddhacca-kukkucca.md), [Doubt (vicikicchā)](../obstacles/vicikiccha.md)

### 3.p107 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p107-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The commentator's own caution: this way of discerning temperament in every detail comes neither in the Pāḷi nor in the Commentary, but only according to the teachers' opinion, so it is not to be relied on as essential. Postures and so on described for the greedy can also be done by the hating and others who dwell diligently; and in a person of mixed temperament the differing marks of posture and so on do not appear together. What is stated in the Commentaries as the way to discern temperament is to be relied on: a teacher with the knowledge of others' minds will know it and give the subject, while another should ask the pupil. Hence one should know either by that knowledge or by asking the person whether he is greedy or of another temperament.

> yasmā pana idaṃ cariyā-vibhāvanavidhānaṃ sabbākā-  
rena neva pāḷiyaṃ, na aṭṭhakathāyaṃ āgataṃ, kevalaṃ  
ācariyamatānusārena vuttaṃ, tasmā na sārato paccetabbaṃ|  
rāgacaritassa hi vuttāni iriyāpathādīni dosacaritādayo pi  
appamādavihārino kātuṃ sakkonti| saṃsaṭṭhacaritassa ca  
puggalassa ekass'; eva bhinnalakkhaṇā iriyāpathādayo na  
sampajjanti|6 yaṃ pan'; etaṃ aṭṭhakathāsu cariyā-vibhā-  
vanavidhānaṃ vuttaṃ, tad eva sārato paccetabbaṃ| vut-  
taṃ h'; etaṃ:- ceto-pariyañāṇassa lābhī ācariyo cariyaṃ  
ñatvā kammaṭṭhānaṃ kathessati, itarena antevāsiko puc-  
chitabbo ti| tasmā cetopariyañāṇena vā taṃ vā puggalaṃ  
pucchitvā jānitabbaṃ: ayaṃ puggalo rāgacarito, ayaṃ  
dosādisu aññataracarito ti|

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p107 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p107-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

What suits each temperament, beginning with lodging (senāsana). For the greedy person the suitable lodging is one of these: one with an unwashed sill standing level with the ground, an unprepared rock overhang, a grass hut, a leaf hut or the like; dusty, full of bats, dilapidated, too high or too low, in bleak surroundings, threatened by dangers, unclean, with an uneven path, where even the bed and stool are full of bugs, ugly and of poor colour, so that merely looking at it brings disgust. (Continued on p. 108.)

> kiṃ caritassa [ca] puggalassa? kiṃ sappāyan7 ti ettha  
pana senāsanaṃ tāva rāgacaritassa adhotavedika-bhūmaṭṭha-  
kaṃ akatapabbhāra-tiṇa-kuṭi-paṇṇasālādīnaṃ aññataraṃ  
rajokiṇṇaṃ jatukābharitaṃ oluggaviluggaṃ ati-uccaṃ vā  
atinīcaṃ vā ujjangalaṃ sāsankaṃ asuciṃ8 visamamaggaṃ,

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p108 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p108"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

What suits the greedy person, continued. Lodging where bed and stool are bug-ridden, ugly and of poor colour, so that the mere sight brings disgust. Inner and outer robes with torn edges, hanging threads, like a net-cake, rough to the touch like hemp, dirty, heavy and hard to keep. A bowl ugly in colour: a clay bowl, or an iron bowl disfigured by pins and knots, heavy, ill-shaped and repulsive like a skull. An alms-route that is disagreeable, not near a village, uneven. An alms-village where people go about as if they did not see him; where, when he leaves a house without getting alms, they say "come, venerable sir", take him into the sitting hall, give gruel and rice, and go off without looking back, as if they had put cattle into a pen. Servers who are slaves or workers, ugly, unsightly, dirty-clothed, foul-smelling, disgusting, who serve gruel and rice disrespectfully as if throwing it away. Gruel, rice and hard food that is rough and ugly: millet, kudrūsa grain, broken rice and the like, stale buttermilk, sour gruel, soup of old vegetables, anything that merely fills the belly. Posture: standing or walking. Object: any of the colour kasiṇas, blue and the rest, that is of impure colour. All this suits the greedy temperament.

> yattha mañcapīṭham pi mangulābharitaṃ1 durūpaṃ dub-  
baṇṇaṃ, yaṃ olokentass'; eva jigucchā uppajjati, tādisaṃ  
sappāyaṃ| nivāsanapāpuranaṃ antacchinnaṃ olambavi-  
lambasuttakākiṇṇaṃ jālapūvasadisaṃ sāṇi viya kharasam-  
phassaṃ kiliṭṭhaṃ bhāritaṃ2 kicchapariharaṇaṃ sappā-  
yaṃ| patto pi dubbaṇṇo mattikā patto vā āṇigaṇṭhikāhato  
ayopatto vā garuko dussaṇṭhāno sīsakapālam iva jeguccho  
vattati| bhikkhācāramaggo pi amanāpo anāsannagāmo  
visamo vaṭṭati| bhikkhācāragāmo pi yattha manussā apas-  
santā viya caranti, yattha ekakule pi bhikkhaṃ alabhitvā  
nikkhamantaṃ: ehi, bhante ti āsanasālaṃ pavesetvā yāgu-  
bhattaṃ datvā gacchantā gāvī3 viya vaje pavesetvā anapalo-  
kento4 gacchanti, tādiso vaṭṭati| parivisakamanussā5 pi  
dāsā vā kammakarā vā dubbaṇṇā duddassikā6 kiliṭṭhavasanā  
duggandhā jigucchā,7 ye acittīkārena yāgubhattaṃ chaḍ-  
ḍentā viya parivisanti, tādisā sappāyā| yāgubhattakhajja-  
kam pi lūkhaṃ dubbaṇṇaṃ sāmāka-kudrūsaka-kaṇājakādim  
ayaṃ pūtitakkaṃ biḷangaṃ jiṇṇasākasūpeyyaṃ yaṃ kiñcid-  
eva kevalaṃ udarapūramattaṃ vaṭṭati| iriyāpatho pi 'ssa  
ṭhānaṃ vā cankamo vā vaṭṭati| ārammaṇaṃ nīlādisu  
vaṇṇakasiṇesu yaṃ kiñci aparisuddhavaṇṇan ti idaṃ rāga-  
caritassa sappāyaṃ|

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p108 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p108-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

What suits the hating person, begun. A lodging neither too high nor too low, with shade and water, with well-divided walls, pillars and stairs, well finished with garland and creeper designs and varied paintings, with a bright, even, smooth and soft floor like a Brahmā mansion, adorned with a canopy of flowers and coloured cloth, with a well-spread, clean and pleasing bed and stool, and fragrant with scent placed here and there: one that at mere sight gives joy and gladness. Such a lodging suits him; and the path to it should be free of all dangers, clean, level and adorned.

> dosacaritassa senāsanaṃ nāti-uccaṃ  
nātinīcaṃ chāyūdakasampannaṃ suvibhattabhittithambha-  
sopānaṃ supariniṭṭhitamālākamma - latākamma8 - nānāvi-  
dhacittakamma - samujjala9 - samasiniddha - mudubhūmitalaṃ  
brahmavimānam iva kusumadāmavicitravaṇṇacelavitānasam-  
alankataṃ supaññatta - sucimanoramattharaṇa - mañcapī-  
ṭham, tattha tattha vāsatthāya nikkhittakusumavāsagandha-  
sugandhaṃ yaṃ dassanamatten'; eva pītipāmojjaṃ janayati,  
evarūpaṃ sappāyaṃ| tassa pana senāsanassa maggo pi  
sabbaparissayavimutto suci samatalo alankatapaṭiyatto va

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p109 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p109"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

What suits the hating person, continued. Furnishing of the lodging not too many (to limit the lodging of bugs, rats and the like): one bed and stool. His robes of the best cloths (silk, fine cotton, linen and so on), light, of one or two layers, suited to a monk, well dyed and clean in colour. His bowl well shaped like a water bubble, polished like a jewel, spotless, an iron bowl of very pure colour. The alms-route free of danger, level, pleasant, not too far and not too near a village; the alms-village where people, saying "the venerable one will come now", arrange a seat in a sprinkled and swept place, come out to meet him, take his bowl, seat him and serve him themselves with respect. His servers good-looking, pleasing, well bathed and anointed, scented with incense and flowers, in varied clean and pleasing clothes and ornaments, respectful. Food of good colour, smell and taste, nourishing, pleasing, in every way excellent, as much as needed. Posture: lying down or sitting. Object: any of the colour kasiṇas of very pure colour. This suits the hating person.

> vaṭṭati| senāsanaparikkhāro p'; ettha kīḷamankuladīghajāti1-  
mūsikānaṃ2 nissayaparicchindanatthaṃ nātibahuko, ekaṃ  
mañcapīṭhamattam eva vaṭṭati| nivāsanapārupanam3 pi  
'ssa cīnapaṭṭa-somārapaṭṭakoseyyakappāsikasukhumakhomā-  
dīnaṃ yaṃ yaṃ paṇītaṃ, tena tena ekapaṭṭaṃ vā dupaṭṭaṃ  
vā sallahukaṃ samaṇasāruppena surattaṃ suddhavaṇṇaṃ4  
vaṭṭati| patto udakabubbuḷam iva susaṇṭhāno maṇi viya  
sumaṭṭho nimmalo samaṇasāruppena suparisuddhavaṇṇo  
ayomayo vaṭṭati| bhikkhācāramaggo parissayavimutto sa-  
mo manāpo nātidūra-nāccāsannagāmo vaṭṭati| bhikkhā-  
cāragāmo pi yattha manussā: idāni ayyo āgamissatī ti sitta-  
sammaṭṭhe padese āsanaṃ paññāpetvā paccuggantvā pattaṃ  
ādāya gharaṃ pavesetvā5 paññattāsane nisīdāpetvā sak-  
kaccaṃ sahatthā parivisanti, tādiso vaṭṭati|  
     parivesakā pan'; assa ye honti abhirūpā pāsādikā sunhātā  
suvilittā dhūmavāsa-6kusumagandhasurabhino nānā virāga-  
sucimanuññavatthā bharaṇapaṭimaṇḍitā sakkaccakārino, tā-  
disā sappāyā| yāgubhattakhajjakam pi vaṇṇagandhara-  
sasampannaṃ ojavantaṃ manoramaṃ sabbākārapaṇītaṃ  
yāvadatthaṃ vaṭṭati| iriyāpatho pi 'ssa seyyā vā nisajjā vā  
vaṭṭati| ārammaṇaṃ nīlādisu vaṇṇakasiṇesu yaṃ kiñci  
suparisuddhavaṇṇan ti idaṃ dosacaritassa sappāyaṃ|

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p109 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p109-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

What suits the deluded person. A lodging facing an open direction, unconfined, where, sitting, the directions are seen open. Among postures, walking. His object should not be small (like a winnowing basket or a saucer): in a confined place the mind falls further into confusion, so a large kasiṇa is suitable. The rest is as said for the hating person.

> hacaritassa senāsanaṃ disāmukhaṃ asambādhaṃ vaṭṭati,  
yattha nisinnassa vivaṭā disā paññāyanti|7 iriyāpathesu  
cankamo vaṭṭati| ārammaṇaṃ pan'; assa parittaṃ suppa-  
mattaṃ sarāvamattaṃ vā khuddakaṃ8 na vaṭṭati| sam-  
bādhasmiṃ hi okāse cittaṃ bhiyyo sammohaṃ āpajjati,  
tasmā vipulaṃ mahākasiṇaṃ vaṭṭati| sesaṃ dosacaritassa  
vuttasadisam evā ti idaṃ mohacaritassa sappāyaṃ|

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p109 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p109-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

For the faithful person everything stated for the hating person suits, and among the objects the places of recollection are suitable. For the intelligent person there is no such thing as "this lodging or the like is unsuitable".

> caritassa sabbam pi dosacaritam hi vuttavidhānaṃ sappāyaṃ|  
ārammaṇesu c'; assa anussatiṭṭhānam pi vaṭṭati| buddhi-  
caritassa senāsanādisu: idaṃ nāma asappāyan ti n'; atthi|

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p110 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p110"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

What suits the speculative person. A lodging that opens toward a direction where, as he sits, pleasant parks, groves and lotus ponds, a succession of villages, towns and districts, and blue-shining mountains can be seen does not suit him, because it becomes a cause of his thoughts running about. So he should live in a lodging such as a deep cave-mouth screened by forest, like the Hatthikucchi Overhang or Mahinda's Cave. A large object is not suitable, for it would be a cause of the running about of thought; a small one is suitable. The rest is as said for the greedy person.

> nisinnassa ārāmavanapokkharaṇīrāmaṇeyyakāni gāmaniga-  
majanapadapaṭipāṭiyā1 nīlobhāsā ca pabbatā paññāyanti,  
taṃ na vaṭṭati| taṃ hi vitakkavidhāvanass'; eva paccayo  
hoti| tasmā gambhīre darīmukhe2 vanapaṭicchanne hatthi-  
kucchipabbhāra-mahindaguhāsadise senāsane vasitabbaṃ|  
ārammaṇam pi 'ssa vipulaṃ na vaṭṭati; tādisaṃ hi vitakka-  
vasena sandhāvanāya3 paccayo hoti, parittaṃ pana vaṭṭati|  
sesaṃ rāgacaritassa vuttasadisam evā ti idaṃ vitakkacaritassa  
sappāyaṃ|

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p110 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p110-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high · _restricted: summary only_

This is the exposition, under "suited to one's own temperament", of the differences, causes, discernment and suitable conditions of the temperaments; but the subject suited to each is not yet shown in full, for that will become clear in the exposition of the next heading. So the forty subjects are to be decided by ten ways: by enumeration, by what brings access or absorption, by kind of jhāna, by transcending, by increase or not, by object, by plane, by taking, by condition, and by suitability to temperament. Enumeration: the forty are ten kasiṇas, ten foulnesses, ten recollections, four divine abidings, four immaterial states, one perception, one defining; and the kasiṇas begin to be listed (earth, water, fire, air, blue, yellow, red, white, light, limited space; then the ten foulnesses: bloated, livid, festering, cut up, gnawed, scattered, hacked and scattered, bleeding, worm-infested, skeleton; the recollections begin with Buddha, Dhamma, Saṅgha, virtue, generosity, deities, death, mindfulness occupied with the body, breathing, peace).

> ayaṃ attano cariyānukulan ti ettha āgatacariyānaṃ pa-  
bhedanidānavibhāvanasappāyaparicchedato vitthāro| na ca  
tāva cariyānukulaṃ kammaṭṭhānaṃ sabbākārena āvikataṃ,  
taṃ hi anantarassa mātikāpadassa vitthāre sayam eva āvi-  
bhavissati| tasmā yaṃ vuttaṃ: cattālīsāya kammaṭṭhānesu  
aññataraṃ4 kammaṭṭhānaṃ gahetvā ti, ettha sankhātanid-  
desato, upacārappanāvahato, jhānappabhedato, samatik-  
kamato, vaḍḍhanāvaḍḍhanato, ārammaṇato, bhūmito, gaha-  
ṇato, paccayato, cariyānukulato ti imehi tāva dasahi  
ākārehi kammaṭṭhānavinicchayo veditabbo|  
                        cattāḷīsa kammaṭṭhānāni  
     tatt sankhātaniddesato ti cattāḷīsāya kammaṭṭhānesu iti  
hi vuttaṃ| tatr'; imāni cattāḷīsa kammaṭṭhānāni:- dasa  
kasiṇā, dasa asubhā, dasa anussatiyo, cattāro brahmavihārā,  
cattāro āruppā, ekā saññā, ekaṃ vavatthānan ti|  
     tattha pathavīkasiṇaṃ, āpokasiṇaṃ, tejokasiṇaṃ, vāyo-  
kasiṇaṃ, nīlakasiṇaṃ, pītakasiṇaṃ, lohitakasiṇaṃ, odātaka-  
siṇaṃ ālokakasiṇaṃ, paricchinnākāsakasiṇan ti ime dasa  
kasiṇā|  
     uddhumātakaṃ, vinīlakaṃ, vipubbakaṃ, vicchiddakaṃ,  
vikkhāyitakaṃ, vikkhittakaṃ, hatavikkhittakaṃ, lohitakaṃ,  
puḷuvakaṃ, aṭṭhikan ti ime dasa asubhā|  
     buddhānussati, dhammānussati, sanghānussati, sīlānussati,  
cāgānussati, devatānussati, maraṇānussati, kāyagatāsati,  
ānāpānasati,5 upasamānussatī ti imā dasa anussatiyo|

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice_

concepts: [The forty meditation subjects (kammaṭṭhāna)](../concepts/forty-kammatthanas.md) · practices: [The kasiṇas](../practices/kasina.md), [Contemplation of the foul (asubha-bhāvanā): summary only](../practices/asubha-bhavana.md), [Recollection of the Buddha](../practices/buddhanussati.md), [Recollection of the Dhamma](../practices/dhammanussati.md), [Recollection of the Saṅgha](../practices/sanghanussati.md), [Recollection of one's virtue](../practices/silanussati.md), [Recollection of one's generosity](../practices/caganussati.md), [Recollection of the deities](../practices/devatanussati.md), [Mindfulness directed to the body (kāyagatāsati): summary only](../practices/kayagatasati.md), [Mindfulness of breathing (ānāpānassati)](../practices/anapanasati.md), [Recollection of peace (upasamānussati)](../practices/upasamanussati.md)

### 3.p111 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p111"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The enumeration of the forty subjects is completed: the four divine abidings (loving-kindness, compassion, gladness, equanimity), the four immaterial states (base of infinite space, of infinite consciousness, of nothingness, of neither perception nor non-perception), the one perception (perception of repulsiveness in nutriment) and the one defining (defining of the four elements).

> mettā, karuṇā, muditā, upekkhā ti ime cattāro brahmavi-  
hārā|  
     ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ, viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ, ākiñcaññāya-  
tanaṃ, nevasaññānāsaññāyatanan ti ime cattāro āruppā|  
     āhāre paṭikūlasaññā1 ekā saññā|  
     catudhātuvavatthānaṃ ekaṃ vavatthānan ti evaṃ sankhā-  
taniddesato vinicchayo veditabbo|

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice_

concepts: [The forty meditation subjects (kammaṭṭhāna)](../concepts/forty-kammatthanas.md), [The four divine abidings (brahmavihāra)](../concepts/four-brahmaviharas.md) · practices: [Development of the four divine abidings (brahmavihāra)](../practices/brahmavihara-bhavana.md), [Defining the four elements (catudhātuvavatthāna)](../practices/catudhatuvavatthana.md)

### 3.p111 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p111-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

Which bring access only and which absorption. Ten subjects bring access only: the eight recollections other than mindfulness occupied with the body and mindfulness of breathing, the perception of repulsiveness in nutriment, and the defining of the four elements; the rest bring absorption. By kind of jhāna: the ten kasiṇas with breathing are of four jhānas (fourfold count); the ten foulnesses with the body-mindfulness are of the first jhāna; the first three divine abidings of three jhānas; the fourth divine abiding and the four immaterial states are, as this edition prints, "catukkajjhānikā". By transcending: in subjects of three or four jhānas there is transcending of factors (applied and sustained thought and so on) with the same objects; in the fourth divine abiding also (joy is left behind); in the four immaterial states there is transcending of the object.

> upacārappanāvahato ti ṭhapetvā kāyagatāsatiñ ca ānāpā-  
nassatiñ ca avasesā aṭṭha anussatiyo, āhāre paṭikūlasaññā,  
catudhātuvavatthānan ti imān'; eva h'; ettha dasa kammaṭ-  
ṭhānāni upacārāvahāni; sesāni appanāvahānī ti evaṃ  
upacārappanāvahato|  
     jhānappabhedato ti appanāvahesu c'; ettha ānāpānasatiyā  
saddhiṃ dasa kasiṇā catukkajjhānikā honti| kāyagatāya  
satiyā saddhiṃ dasa asubhā paṭhamajjhānikā| purimā tayo  
brahmavihārā tikajjhānikā| catutthabrahmavihāro cattāro  
ca āruppā catukkajjhānikā ti evaṃ jhānappabhedato|  
     samatikkamato ti dve samatikkamā: angasamatikkamo ca  
ārammaṇasamatikkamo ca| tattha sabbesu pi tika-catuk-  
kajjhānikesu kammaṭṭhānesu angasamatikkamo hoti, vitak-  
kavicārādīni jhānangāni samatikkamitvā te svev'; āramma-  
ṇesu dutiyajjhānādīnaṃ pattabbato; tathā catutthabrahma-  
vihāre| so pi hi mettādīnaṃ yeva ārammaṇe somanassaṃ  
samatikkamitvā pattabbo ti| catūsu pana āruppesu āram-  
maṇasamatikkamo hoti| purimesu hi navasu kasiṇesu añña-  
taraṃ samatikkamitvā ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ pattabbaṃ| ākā-  
sādīni ca samatikkamitvā viññāṇañcāyatanādīni| sesesu  
samatikkamo natthī ti evaṃ samatikkamato|

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice_

### 3.p111 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p111-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high · _restricted: summary only_

Increase. Of the forty, the ten kasiṇas alone are to be enlarged: as far as the kasiṇa spreads, within that region one can hear sound with the divine ear, see forms with the divine eye, and know others' minds with mind. Mindfulness occupied with the body and the foulnesses are not to be enlarged, because they are delimited by their place and there is no benefit in it.

> vaḍḍhanāvaḍḍhanato ti imesu cattāḷīsāya kammaṭṭhānesu  
dasakasiṇā neva vaḍḍhetabbāni| yattakaṃ hi okāsaṃ  
kasiṇena pharati, tad-abbhantare dibbāya sotadhātuyā sad-  
daṃ sotuṃ, dibbena cakkhunā rūpāni passituṃ, parasattānañ  
ca cetasā cittam aññātuṃ samattho hoti| kāyagatā sati  
pana asubhāni ca na vaḍḍhetabbāni| kasmā? okāsena  
paricchinnattā2 ānisaṃsābhāvā ca| sā ca nesaṃ okāsena  
paricchinnatā bhāvanānaye āvibhavissati|

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice_

practices: [The kasiṇas](../practices/kasina.md), [Contemplation of the foul (asubha-bhāvanā): summary only](../practices/asubha-bhavana.md), [Mindfulness directed to the body (kāyagatāsati): summary only](../practices/kayagatasati.md)

### 3.p112 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p112"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high · _restricted: summary only_

Why the foulnesses are not to be enlarged: if they are enlarged only a heap of corpses grows and there is no benefit. The Sopāka question is cited: the perception of form is clear (through enlarging), that of bones unclear (not enlarged); and the statement that the bone-perception "covers the earth" refers to the way it appears to one who has the gain of it, not to enlarging.

> ḍhitesu kuṇaparāsi yeva vaḍḍhati, na koci ānisaṃso atthi|  
vuttam pi c'etaṃ sopākapañhavyākaraṇe1:- vibhūtā bha-  
gavā-rūpasaññā, avibhūtā aṭṭhikasaññā ti| tatra hi nimit-  
tavaḍḍhanavasena rūpasaññā vibhūtā ti vuttā, aṭṭhika-  
saññā avaḍḍhanavasena avibhūtā ti [vuttā2]| yaṃ pan'  
etaṃ kevalaṃ aṭṭhikasaññāya aphariṃ pathaviṃ iman ti  
vuttaṃ, taṃ lābhissa sato upaṭṭhānākāravasena vuttaṃ|

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice_

practices: [Contemplation of the foul (asubha-bhāvanā): summary only](../practices/asubha-bhavana.md)

### 3.p112 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p112-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The others too are not to be enlarged. Breathing: enlarging the breath only enlarges a mass of wind, it is delimited by its place, and has a drawback. The divine abidings have beings as their object, so enlarging the sign would only enlarge a mass of beings, and no benefit comes from it. The phrase "having pervaded one direction with a mind accompanied by loving-kindness" is said by way of delimiting (pariggaha) the beings.

> ānisaṃsābhāvā pana na vaḍḍhetab-  
bānī ti| yathā ca etāni, evaṃ sesāni pi na vaḍḍhetabbāni|  
kasmā ? tesu hi ānāpānanimittaṃ tāva vaḍḍhayato vāta-  
rāsiye vaḍḍhati, okāsena ca paricchinnaṃ| iti sādīnavattā  
okāsena ca paricchinnattā na vaḍḍhetabbaṃ| brahmavihārā  
sattārammaṇā, tesaṃ nimittaṃ vaḍḍhayato sattarāsi yeva  
vaḍḍheyya, na ca tena attho atthi; tasmā tāni pi na vaḍḍhe-  
tabbaṃ| yaṃ pana vuttaṃ: mettāsahagatena cetasā ekaṃ  
disaṃ pharitvā ti ādi,3 taṃ pariggahavasen'; eva4 vuttaṃ|

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice_

practices: [Mindfulness of breathing (ānāpānassati)](../practices/anapanasati.md), [Development of the four divine abidings (brahmavihāra)](../practices/brahmavihara-bhavana.md)

### 3.p113 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p113"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

In the divine abidings there is no counterpart sign to enlarge; limited and measureless are understood by way of delimiting. Among the immaterial objects, space is only the removal of the kasiṇa, so it is attended to by way of the kasiṇa's removal, and to enlarge beyond that gives nothing; consciousness is an intrinsic-nature state that cannot be enlarged; the disappearance of consciousness is only its absence; the base of neither perception nor non-perception is a state of intrinsic nature and not to be enlarged. The other subjects (Buddha-recollection and so on) have no counterpart sign, so nothing to enlarge.

> nimittaṃ vaḍḍhento| paṭibhāganimittam eva c'; ettha n'; atthi,  
yad ayaṃ [yogī1] vaḍḍheyya| paritta-appamāṇārammaṇatā  
p'; ettha pariggahavasen'; eva veditabbā|  
     āruppārammaṇesu pi ākāsaṃ kasiṇugghāṭimattā2; taṃ  
hi kasiṇāpagamavasen'; eva manasikātabbaṃ| tato paraṃ  
vaḍḍhayato3 na kiñci hoti| viññāṇaṃ sabhāvadhammattā;  
na hi sakkā sabhāvadhammaṃ vaḍḍhetuṃ| viññāṇapa-  
gamo viññāṇassa abhāvamattattā| nevasaññānāsaññāya-  
tanārammaṇaṃ sabhāvadhammattā yeva na vaḍḍhetabbaṃ|  
sesāni animittattā| paṭibhāganimittaṃ hi vaḍḍhetabbaṃ  
nāma bhaveyya| buddhānussati-ādīnañ ca neva paṭibhāga-  
nimittaṃ ārammaṇaṃ hoti, tasmā taṃ na vaḍḍhetabban ti  
evaṃ vaḍḍhanāvaḍḍhanato|

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice_

practices: [Development of the four divine abidings (brahmavihāra)](../practices/brahmavihara-bhavana.md)

### 3.p113 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p113-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

By object and by plane. Twenty-two subjects have a counterpart sign as object (ten kasiṇas, ten foulnesses, breathing, body-mindfulness); twelve have intrinsic-nature states; six have objects not to be described. Eight have a moving (calita) object, but only at the preparatory stage (the festering, bleeding and worm-infested foulnesses, breathing, the water, fire and air kasiṇas, and the circle of light of the sun and the like in the light kasiṇa); the counterpart sign is still. By plane: the ten foulnesses, body-mindfulness and the perception of repulsiveness in nutriment (twelve) do not occur among the devas; these twelve with breathing (thirteen) do not occur in the Brahma world; in the immaterial realm nothing occurs but the four immaterial states; among humans all occur.

> ārammaṇato ti imesu ca cattāḷīsāya kammaṭṭhānesu dasa  
kasiṇā, dasa asubhā, ānāpānasati,4 kāyagatāsatī ti imāni  
dvāvīsati paṭibhāganimittārammaṇāni, sesāni na paṭibhā-  
ganimittārammaṇāni| tathā dasasu anussatīsu ṭhapetvā  
ānāpānasatiñ ca kāyagatāsatiñ ca avasesā aṭṭha anussatiyo,  
āhāre paṭikūlasaññā, catudhātuvavatthānaṃ, viññāṇañ-  
cāyatanaṃ, nevasaññānāsaññāyatanan ti imāni dvādasa  
sabhāvadhammārammaṇāni| dasa kasiṇā, dasa asubhā,  
ānāpānasati, kāyagatāsatī ti imāni dvāvīsati nimittāramma-  
ṇāni, sesāni cha na vattabbārammaṇāni| tathā vipubba-  
kaṃ, lohitakaṃ, puḷuvakaṃ, ānāpānasati, āpokasiṇaṃ, tejo-  
kasiṇaṃ, vāyokasiṇaṃ, yañ ca ālokakasiṇesu suriyādīnaṃ  
obhāsamaṇḍalārammaṇan ti5 imāni aṭṭha calitārammaṇāni,  
tāni ca kho pubbabhāge| paṭibhāgaṃ pana sannisinnam  
eva hoti| sesāni na calitārammaṇānī ti evaṃ ārammaṇato|  
     bhūmito ti ettha ca dasa asubhā, kāyagatāsati, āhāre paṭi-  
kūlasaññā ti6 imāni dvādasa devesu nappavattanti| tāni  
dvādasa ānāpānasati cāti imāni terasa brahmaloke nap-  
pavattanti| arūpabhave pana ṭhapetvā, cattāro āruppe  
aññaṃ nappavattati| manussesu sabbāni pi pavattantī ti--  
evaṃ bhūmito|

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice_

### 3.p114 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p114"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high · _restricted: summary only_

Taking (gahaṇa): by seeing, touching or hearing. The nine kasiṇas other than the air kasiṇa and the ten foulnesses (nineteen) are to be taken by sight: in the preliminary stage the sign is taken by looking with the eyes. In mindfulness occupied with the body the five skin-pentad parts are taken by sight, the rest by hearing; breathing by touch; the air kasiṇa by sight and touch; the remaining eighteen by hearing. The equanimity divine abiding and the four immaterial states are not to be taken by a beginner (ādikammika); the other thirty-five may be.

> gahaṇato ti diṭṭhaphuṭṭhasutaggahaṇato p'; ettha vinicchayo  
veditabbo| tatra ṭhapetvā vāyokasiṇaṃ sesā nava kasiṇā,  
dasa asubhā ti imāni ekūnavīsati diṭṭhena gahetabbāni|  
pubbabhāge cakkhunā oloketvā1 nimittaṃ nesaṃ gahetab-  
ban ti attho| kāyagatāsatiyaṃ tacapañcakaṃ diṭṭhena,  
sesaṃ sutenā ti evaṃ tassā ārammaṇaṃ diṭṭhasutena gahe-  
tabbaṃ| ānāpānasati phuṭṭhena, vāyokasiṇaṃ diṭṭhaphuṭ-  
ṭhena, sesāni aṭṭhārasa sutena gahetabbāni, upekkhābrahma-  
vihāro cattāro aruppā ti imāni c'ettha na ādikammikena  
gahetabbāni; sesāni pañcatiṃsa gahetabbānī ti

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice_

practices: [The kasiṇas](../practices/kasina.md), [Mindfulness of breathing (ānāpānassati)](../practices/anapanasati.md), [Mindfulness directed to the body (kāyagatāsati): summary only](../practices/kayagatasati.md)

### 3.p114 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p114-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Condition (paccaya). The nine kasiṇas other than the space kasiṇa are conditions for the immaterial states; the ten kasiṇas for the direct-knowledges; the first three divine abidings for the fourth; each lower immaterial state for the one above; the base of neither perception nor non-perception for the attainment of cessation; and all of them are conditions for a pleasant abiding, for insight and for fortunate existence.

> paccayato ti imesu pana kammaṭṭhānesu, ṭhapetvā ākā-  
sakasiṇaṃ, sesā nava kasiṇā āruppānaṃ paccayā honti,  
dasa pi2 kasiṇā abhiññānaṃ, tayo brahmavihārā catuttha-  
brahmavihārassa, heṭṭhimaṃ heṭṭhimaṃ āruppaṃ upari-  
massa uparimassa, nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ nirodhasamā-  
pattiyā, sabbāni pi sukhavihāravipassanā bhavasampattīnan

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: intermediate · types: practice_

concepts: [The forty meditation subjects (kammaṭṭhāna)](../concepts/forty-kammatthanas.md)

### 3.p114 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p114-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high · _restricted: summary only_

Suitability to temperament, as the text sets it out. For the greedy temperament eleven subjects suit: the ten foulnesses and mindfulness occupied with the body. For the hating temperament eight: the four divine abidings and the four colour kasiṇas. For the deluded and the speculative temperaments, one only: mindfulness of breathing. For the faithful, the first six recollections. For the intelligent, four: mindfulness of death, the recollection of peace, the defining of the four elements, and the perception of repulsiveness in nutriment. The remaining kasiṇas and the four immaterial states suit all temperaments. Among the kasiṇas any small one suits the speculative and any measureless one the deluded.

> cariyānukulato ti cariyānaṃ anukulato p'; ettha vinicchayo  
veditabbo| seyyathīdaṃ, rāgacaritassa tāva:- ettha dasa  
asubhā kāyagatāsatī ti ekādasa kammaṭṭhānāni anukulāni|  
dosacaritassa cattāro brahmavihārā cattāri vaṇṇakasiṇānī ti  
aṭṭha| mohacaritassa ca2 vitakkacaritassa ca ekaṃ ānāpāna-  
sati-kammaṭṭhānam eva| saddhācaritassa purimā cha anus-  
satiyo| buddhicaritassa maraṇasati upasamānussati, catu-  
dhātuvavatthānaṃ, āhāre paṭikūlasaññā ti cattāri| sesakasi-  
ṇāni cattāro ca āruppā sabbacaritānaṃ anukulāni| kasiṇesu  
ca yaṃ kiñci parittaṃ vitakkacaritassa, appamāṇaṃ moha-  
caritassā ti evam ettha cariyānukulato vinicchayo veditabbo

### 3.p114 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p114-4"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The commentator's qualification: all this is said by way of direct opposition and of extreme suitability; there is no wholesome development that does not suppress greed and the rest, or that does not help faith and the rest. He quotes the Meghiya-sutta: four things are to be developed further: the foul is to be developed for abandoning greed; loving-kindness for abandoning ill will; mindfulness of breathing (for cutting off thinking, completed on the next page).

> sabbañ c'; etaṃ ujuvipaccanīkavasena ca atisappāya-  
vasena ca vuttaṃ| rāgādīnaṃ pana avikkhambhikā sad-  
dhādīnaṃ vā anupakārā kusalabhāvanā nāma n'; atthi|  
vuttam pi c'; etaṃ meghiyasutte:- cattāro dhammā uttari3  
bhāvetabbā: asubhā bhāvetabbā rāgassa pahānāya, mettā bhāve-  
tabbā vyāpādassa pahānāya ānāpānasati bhāvetabbā vitak-

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice_

terms: [carita (cariyā)](../terms/carita.md)

### 3.p115 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-3-p115"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high · _restricted: summary only_

(Meghiya-sutta, concluded) mindfulness of breathing is for cutting off thinking (vitakka), and the perception of impermanence is for uprooting the conceit "I am" (asmimāna). In the Rāhula-sutta seven subjects are given to one person ("develop loving-kindness, Rāhula"). Therefore one should not cling to the mere wording but seek the intention everywhere.

> kupacchedāya, aniccasaññā bhāvetabbā asmimānassa1 samag-  
ghātāyā2 ti| rāhula-sutte pi:- mettaṃ, rāhula, bhāvanaṃ  
bhāvetū ti3 ādinā nayena ekass'; eva satta kammaṭṭhānāni  
vuttāni; tasmā vacanamatte abhinivesaṃ akatvā sabbattha  
adhippāyo pariyesitabbo ti|
