### 20.p637 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-20-p637"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

When one of these arises the meditator thinks "such knowledge, such rapture, tranquillity, happiness, resolve, exertion, establishment, equanimity, delight has never arisen in me before; surely I have reached the path, reached the fruit", and takes what is not the path to be the path and what is not the fruit to be the fruit. For one who so takes it, the course of insight is lost (ukkantā); he sets aside his own basic subject and sits savouring the delight. The illumination and the rest are called imperfections because they are grounds (vatthu) of imperfection, not because they are unwholesome; delight is both an imperfection and a ground of imperfection. They are ten by ground and thirty by grasp: taking "my illumination has arisen" is grasping by view; "agreeable illumination has arisen" grasping by conceit; savouring it grasping by craving; so for each of the ten. Through them an unskilled, undiscerning meditator wavers and is distracted, regarding each as "this is mine, this I am, this is my self". The ancients say the mind trembles at illumination, knowledge, rapture, tranquillity, happiness, resolve, exertion, establishment, equanimity-and-adverting, and delight. The skilful, wise, discerning meditator, when they arise, thinks: "this illumination has arisen in me; but it is impermanent, conditioned, dependently arisen, of a nature to end, to pass, to fade, to cease"; or, "if illumination were the self, it would be right to take it as self; but taken as self, it is not-self, since it is not under control, impermanent because it is not after having been, and suffering since it is oppressed by arising and passing", as is set out in the seven-fold contemplation.

> na sakkā hoti| yathā ca okāse, evaṃ etesu pi aññatarasmiṃ  
uppanne yogāvacaro: na vata me ito pubbe evarūpaṃ ñāṇaṃ  
uppannapubbam | | | evarūpā pīti | | | passaddhi, sukhaṃ,  
adhimokkho, paggaho, upaṭṭhānaṃ, upekkhā, nikanti uppan-  
napubbā! addhā maggappatto 'smi! phalappatto 'smīti  
amaggam eva maggo ti, aphalam eva ca phalan ti gaṇhāti|  
tassa amaggaṃ maggo ti, aphalaṃ phalan ti gaṇhato vipas-  
sanāvīthi ukkantā nāma hoti| so attano mūlakammaṭṭhā-  
naṃ vissajjetvā nikantim eva assādento nisīdatī ti|  
     ettha ca obhāsādayo upakkilesavatthutāya upakkilesā ti  
vuttā, na akusalattā| nikanti pana upakkileso c'; eva upak-  
kilesavatthu ca| vatthuvasen'; eva c'; ete dasa; gāhavasena  
pana samatiṃsa honti| kathaṃ? mama obhāso uppanno!  
ti gaṇhato hi diṭṭhigāho hoti| manāpo vata obhāso uppanno!  
ti gaṇhato mānagāho, obhāsaṃ assādayato taṇhāgāho, iti  
obhāse ditthimānataṇhāvasena tayo gāhā| tathā sesesu  
pī ti evaṃ gāhavasena samatiṃsa upakkilesā honti| tesaṃ  
vasena akusalo avyatto yogāvacaro obhāsādisu kampati  
vikkhipati, obhāsādisu ekekaṃ: etaṃ mama, eso 'ham asmi,  
eso me attā ti samanupassati| ten'; āhu porāṇā:-  
          obhāse ceva ñāṇe ca pītiyā ca vikampati,  
          passaddhiyā sukhe ceva yehi cittaṃ pavedhati|  
          adhimokkhe ca paggāhe upaṭṭhāne ca kampati  
          upekkhāvajjanāya ca upekkhāya nikantiyā ti|  
     kusalo pana paṇḍito vyatto buddhisampanno yogā-  
vacaro obhāsādisu uppannesu: ayaṃ kho me obhāso  
uppanno! so kho pan'; āyaṃ anicco sankhato paṭiccasamup-  
panno khayadhammo vayadhammo virāgadhammo nirodha-  
dhammo ti iti vā taṃ paññāya paricchindati upaparikkhati,  
atha vā pan'; assa evaṃ hoti: sace obhāso attā bhaveyya| attā  
ti gahetuṃ vaṭṭeyya| anattā ca pan'; āyaṃ attā ti gahito,  
tasmā so avasavattanaṭṭhena anattā, hutvā abhāvaṭṭhena  
anicco, uppādavayapaṭipīḷanaṭṭhena dukkho ti sabbaṃ arū-  
pasattake vuttanayena1 vitthāretabbaṃ| yathā ca obhāse,  
evaṃ sesesu pi| so evaṃ upaparikkhitvā obhāsaṃ: n'; etaṃ  
mama, n'; eso 'ham asmi, na me so attā ti samanupassati | | |

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge, meditation · stage: intermediate · types: powers-experiences, consciousness-mind_

terms: [vipassanūpakkilesa](../terms/vipassanupakkilesa.md)

### 20.p638 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-20-p638"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Concluding the imperfections: he regards illumination, knowledge and the rest as "this is not mine, I am not this, this is not my self", and so regarding does not waver or tremble at them. The ancients say: one whose understanding is practised in these ten spots is skilled in restlessness about the Dhamma and is not distracted. So not distracted, he unpicks the thirty-fold tangle of imperfections and defines the path and the not-path: illumination and the rest are not the path; the insight-knowledge that has entered the true course, free of imperfections, is the path. The knowledge that stands, knowing the path and the not-path, is the purification by knowledge and vision of what is path and not-path. At this point he has defined the three truths: by defining mind-and-matter in the purification of view he has defined suffering; by discerning conditions in the purification by overcoming doubt, the origin; by determining the right path here, the path. The chapter ends with the colophon, the twentieth chapter.

> ñāṇaṃ |||pe||| nikantiṃ: n'; etaṃ mama, n'; eso 'ham  
asmi, na me so attā ti samanupassati| evaṃ samanupassanto  
obhāsādisu na kampati na vedhati| ten'; āhu porāṇā:-  
          imāni dasa ṭhānāni paññā yassa parīcitā,  
          dhammuddhaccakusalo hoti na ca vikkhepam gacchatī ti|  
     so evaṃ vikkhepaṃ agacchanto taṃ samatiṃsavidhaṃ  
upakkilesajaṭaṃ vijaṭetvā: obhāsādayo dhammā na maggo,  
upakkilesavimuttaṃ pana vīthipaṭipannaṃ vipassanāñāṇam  
maggo ti maggañ ca amaggañ ca vavatthapeti| tass'; evaṃ  
ayaṃ maggo, ayaṃ na maggo ti maggañ ca amaggañ ca  
ñatvā ṭhitaṃ ñāṇaṃ maggāmaggañāṇadassanavisuddhī ti  
veditabbaṃ|  
                     [tiṇṇaṃ saccānaṃ vavatthānaṃ]  
     ettāvatā ca pana tena tiṇṇaṃ saccānaṃ vavatthānaṃ  
kataṃ hoti| kathaṃ? diṭṭhivisuddhiyaṃ tāva nāma-  
rūpassa vavatthāpanena, dukkhasaccassa vavatthānaṃ  
kataṃ; kankhāvitaraṇavisuddhiyaṃ paccayapariggahaṇena  
samudayasaccassa vavatthānaṃ, imissa maggāmaggañā-  
ṇadassanavisuddhiyaṃ sammāmaggassa avadhāraṇena magga-  
saccassa vavatthānaṃ katan ti| evaṃ lokiyen'; eva tāva  
ñāṇena tiṇṇaṃ saccānaṃ vavatthānaṃ kataṃ hoti|  
iti sādhujanapāmojjatthāya kate visuddhi-magge paññā-  
bhāvanādhikāre maggāmaggañāṇadassanavisuddhiniddeso  
nāma vīsatimo paricchedo|

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge, meditation · stage: intermediate · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [vipassanūpakkilesa](../terms/vipassanupakkilesa.md)

### 21 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-21"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Purification by knowledge and vision of the way is insight reaching its culmination in eight knowledges, with conformity knowledge as the ninth: knowledge of contemplating rise and fall, of dissolution, of appearance as terror, of danger, of dispassion, of desire for deliverance, of reflection, of equanimity about formations, and conformity with truth.

> aṭṭhannaṃ pana ñāṇānaṃ vasena sikhāppattā vipassanā navamañ ca saccānulomikañāṇan ti ayaṃ paṭipadāñāṇadassanavisuddhi nāma.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: practice_

terms: [udayabbayānupassanā-ñāṇa](../terms/udayabbaya-nana.md), [bhaṅgānupassanā-ñāṇa](../terms/bhanga-nana.md), [saṅkhārupekkhā-ñāṇa](../terms/sankharupekkha.md), [anuloma-ñāṇa](../terms/anuloma-nana.md) · concepts: [The insight knowledges (vipassanā-ñāṇa)](../concepts/insight-knowledges.md)

### 21 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-21-2"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

As he repeatedly sees all formations breaking up, the knowledge of dissolution gains strength with eight benefits: abandoning of the view of existence, giving up attachment to life, constant application, purified livelihood, no anxiety, no fear, patience and gentleness, and conquest of aversion and delight; 'who looks on the world as a bubble or a mirage, the King of Death does not see'.

> bhavadiṭṭhippahānaṃ, ussukkappahānaṃ, jīvitanikanti-pariccāgo, vigatabhayatā, sadā yuttapayuttatā, khanti-soraccapaṭilābho …

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: practice, death-dying_

concepts: [The insight knowledges (vipassanā-ñāṇa)](../concepts/insight-knowledges.md)

### 21 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-21-3"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Simile of the burning house: a householder asleep after his meal is the fool grasping the aggregates as 'I, mine'; waking and seeing the fire and being afraid is knowledge of appearance as terror; looking for the way out is desire for deliverance; seeing the way is conformity; going out is change-of-lineage; going quickly is the path; standing in a safe place is fruition.

> nikkhamanamaggaṃ olokanaṃ viya muñcitukamyatā ñāṇaṃ; maggadassanaṃ viya anulomaṃ; nikkhamanaṃ viya gotrabhūñāṇaṃ; vegena gamanaṃ viya maggañāṇaṃ; khemaṭṭhāne ṭhānaṃ viya phalañāṇaṃ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: practice, karma-liberation_

terms: [gotrabhū](../terms/gotrabhu.md), [magga](../terms/magga.md), [phala](../terms/phala.md) · concepts: [The insight knowledges (vipassanā-ñāṇa)](../concepts/insight-knowledges.md)

### 21 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-21-4"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

The three gateways to liberation lead out of the world: seeing all formations as limited (by rise and fall) the mind enters the signless element; by stirring up the mind about formations it enters the desireless; seeing all dhammas as alien it enters the void.

> tīṇi kho pan' imāni vimokkhamukhāni lokaniyyānāya saṃvattanti. sabbasaṅkhāre paricchedaparivaṭumato samanupassanatāya animittāya ca dhātuyā cittasampakkhandanatāya …

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation_

terms: [animitta](../terms/animitta.md), [appaṇihita](../terms/appanihita.md), [suññatā](../terms/sunnata.md) · concepts: [The three gateways to liberation (vimokkha-mukha)](../concepts/three-doors-of-liberation.md)

### 21 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-21-5"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Insight leading to emergence determines the character of the path: in the dry-insight worker, and in one with attainments who arises from no jhāna as basis or from the first jhāna, the path arising has the first-jhāna factors.

> vipassanāniyamena hi sukkhavipassakassa uppannamaggo pi samāpattilābhino jhānaṃ pādakaṃ akatvā, uppannamaggo pi paṭhamajjhānaṃ pādakaṃ katvā …

_level: ultimate · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: practice, dispute_

terms: [sukkhavipassaka](../terms/sukkhavipassaka.md) · concepts: [The two vehicles: calm-first and pure insight](../concepts/two-vehicles.md) · disputes: [Must jhāna precede insight, or can insight proceed on momentary concentration (dry insight)?](../debates/dry-insight-or-jhana-first.md)

### 21 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-21-6"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Conformity knowledge conforms both to the eight insight knowledges before it and to the thirty-seven requisites of awakening after it, as a king conforms both to the verdicts of his eight judges and to the ancient royal law.

> rājā viya hi anulomañāṇaṃ; aṭṭha vohārikamahāmattā viya aṭṭha ñāṇāni; porāṇo rājadhammo viya sattatiṃsa bodhipakkhiyā.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: practice_

terms: [anuloma-ñāṇa](../terms/anuloma-nana.md), [bodhipakkhiya dhammā](../terms/bodhipakkhiya.md) · concepts: [The insight knowledges (vipassanā-ñāṇa)](../concepts/insight-knowledges.md)

### 22 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-22"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence moderate

Change-of-lineage knowledge takes nibbāna as object and passes from the worldling lineage to the noble one; then the path arises. The first path abandons personality view, doubt and clinging to rules and observances; the second attenuates sensual lust and ill will; the third abandons them; the fourth abandons lust for form and the formless, conceit, restlessness and ignorance.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: realized · types: karma-liberation_

terms: [gotrabhū](../terms/gotrabhu.md), [saṁyojana](../terms/samyojana.md), [magga](../terms/magga.md) · concepts: [The four paths and four fruits](../concepts/four-paths-and-fruits.md)

### 22 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-22-2"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Each stream-enterer, once-returner and non-returner has five kinds of reviewing — of path, fruit, nibbāna, defilements abandoned and defilements remaining — and the arahant four, since none remain: nineteen kinds of reviewing in all, as the maximum; a trainee may or may not review the defilements abandoned and remaining.

> evaṃ sabbāni pi ekūnavīsati paccavekkhaṇāni nāma, ukkaṭṭhaparicchedo yeva c' eso.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: realized · types: karma-liberation_

terms: [paccavekkhaṇa](../terms/paccavekkhana.md) · concepts: [Reviewing knowledge (paccavekkhaṇa-ñāṇa)](../concepts/reviewing-knowledge.md)

### 22 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-22-3"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Abandoning by substitution of opposites: defining mentality-materiality abandons personality view; discerning conditions abandons the views of no cause and of wrong cause and the stain of doubt; comprehension by groups abandons the grasping of a mass as 'I' and 'mine'; defining the path and not-path abandons the perception of a path in what is not the path.

> nāmarūpaparicchedena tāva sakkāyadiṭṭhiyā, paccayapariggahena ahetu-visamahetudiṭṭhiyā ceva kaṅkhāmalassa ca, kalāpasammasanena: ahaṃ, mamā ti samūhagāhassa, maggāmaggavavatthānena amagge maggasaññāya …

_level: ultimate · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, practice_

terms: [pahāna](../terms/pahana.md) · concepts: [The kinds of abandoning (pahāna)](../concepts/five-kinds-of-abandoning.md)

_Superseded by [22.p694](visuddhimagga.md#tea-visuddhimagga-22-p694)_

### 22.p682 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-22-p682"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

At the moment of the arahat path he emerges from the remaining fetters and latent tendencies (from lust for fine-material and immaterial existence, conceit, restlessness, ignorance; the latent conceit, latent lust for existence, latent ignorance), from the defilements that follow them and the aggregates, and outwardly from all signs: as quoted, "understanding in the turning away by emerging in both ways is knowledge in the path". In the development of the eight worldly attainments calm is stronger; in developing the contemplation of impermanence and so on, insight is stronger; in the moment of the noble path they occur yoked together, neither exceeding the other; so in the four knowledges of the paths there is a joining of both powers. The text quotes the Paṭisambhidā: for one emerging from the defilements accompanied by restlessness and from the aggregates, one-pointedness of mind, non-distraction, is concentration whose domain is cessation; for one emerging from the defilements accompanied by ignorance, insight in the sense of contemplation has cessation as domain; thus in the sense of emerging calm and insight are of one taste, yoked and not exceeding each other. Then, under "what is to be abandoned by which, and the abandoning", the text begins: in these four knowledges one should know what states are to be abandoned by each knowledge, states that come as fetters, defilements, wrongnesses, worldly conditions, avarice, perversions, ties, wrong courses, taints, floods, yokes, hindrances, adherences, clingings, latent tendencies, stains, unwholesome courses of action and unwholesome thoughts. Fetters are ten things that bind beings to aggregates, to the fruit of kamma and to suffering; of them five are higher fetters (lust for fine-material existence, for immaterial existence, conceit, restlessness, ignorance) (continued on p. 683).

concepts: [The ten fetters](../concepts/ten-fetters.md)

### 22.p683 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-22-p683"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The five lower fetters are personality view (sakkāyadiṭṭhi), doubt (vicikicchā), adherence to rites and observances, sensual lust and ill will (paṭigha), which bind to lower aggregates and the like. Defilements (kilesa), being themselves defiled and defiling their associated states, are ten: greed, hate, delusion, conceit, view, doubt, sloth, restlessness, shamelessness and fearlessness of wrongdoing. Wrongnesses (micchatta) are eight: wrong view, wrong intention, wrong speech, wrong action, wrong livelihood, wrong effort, wrong mindfulness and wrong concentration, or ten with wrong release and wrong knowledge. Worldly conditions (lokadhamma) are eight: gain and loss, fame and disrepute, pleasure and pain, blame and praise; here the text says liking for what is gained and resentment for what is not gained are taken under those. Avarice (macchariya) is fivefold: over dwelling, family, gain, teaching and praise. Perversions (vipallāsa) are three: of perception, of mind and of view, taking the impermanent, painful, not-self and unlovely as permanent, pleasant, self and beautiful. Ties (gantha) are four: covetousness, ill will, adherence to rites and observances, and the insistence that "this alone is truth". Wrong courses (agati) are doing what should not be done and not doing what should be done through desire, hate, delusion and fear. Taints (āsava) is a name for sensual lust, lust for existence, wrong view and ignorance, so called because they flow out (as far as change-of-lineage and the top of existence by way of object, or through the unguarded doors like water through the holes of a jar, in the sense of constant trickling), or because they make the suffering of the round flow (continued on p. 684).

> kāyadiṭṭhi, vicikicchā, sīlabbataparāmāso, kāmarāgo, paṭi-  
gho ti ime pañca adhonibbattakakhandhādisaṃ yojakattā  
adhobhāgiyasaṃyojanāni nāma|  
     kilesā ti sayaṃ sankiliṭṭhattā sampayuttadhammānañ ca  
sankilesikattā lobho, doso, moho, māno, diṭṭhi, vicikicchā,  
thīnaṃ, uddhaccaṃ, ahitikaṃ, anottappan ti ime dasa  
dhammā|  
     micchattā ti micchā pavattanato micchādiṭṭhi, micchāsan-  
kappo, micchāvācā, micchākammanto, micchā-ājīvo, micchā-  
vāyāmo, micchāsati, micchāsamādhī ti ime aṭṭha dhammā|  
micchāviratti-micchāñāṇehi vā saddhiṃ dasa|  
     lokadhammā ti lokappavattiyā sati anuparamadhammattā1  
lābho, alābho, yaso, ayaso,2 sukhaṃ, dukkhaṃ, nindā, pasaṃsā  
ti ime aṭṭha| idha pana kāraṇūpacārena3 lābhādi-vatthu-  
kassa anunayassa alābhādi-vatthukassa paṭighassa c'; etaṃ  
lokadhammagahaṇena gahaṇaṃ katan ti veditabbaṃ|  
     macchariyānī ti āvāsamacchariyaṃ, kulamacchariyaṃ,  
lābhamacchariyaṃ, dhammamacchariyaṃ, vaṇṇamacchari-  
yan ti imāsu āvāsādisu aññesam sādhāraṇabhāvaṃ asahaṇā-  
kārena pavattāni pañca macchariyāni|  
     vipallāsā ti anicca-dukkha-anatta-asubhesu yeva vatthusu  
niccaṃ sukhaṃ attā subhan ti evaṃ pavatto saññāvipallāso,  
cittavipallāso, diṭṭhivipallāso ti ime tayo|  
     ganthā ti nāma kāyassa ceva rūpakāyassa ca ganthanato  
abhijjhādayo cattāro| tathā hi te abhijjhā-kāyagantho,  
vyāpādo-kāyagantho, sīlabbataparāmāso-kāyagantho, idaṃ-  
saccābhiniveso-kāyagantho icc'; eva vuttā|  
     agatī ti chanda-dosa-moha-bhayehi akattabbakaraṇassa  
kattabbākaraṇassa ca adhivacanaṃ| taṃ hi ariyehi agan-  
tabbattā agatī ti vuccati|  
     āsavā ti ārammaṇavasena āgotrabhūto ābhavaggato ca  
savanā-asaṃvutehi vā dvārehi ghaṭachiddehi, udakaṃ viya  
savanato niccapaggharaṇ'; aṭṭhena, saṃsāradukkhassa vā  
savanato kāmarāgabhavarāgamicchādiṭṭhi-āvijjānam etaṃ  
adhivacanaṃ|

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind_

concepts: [The ten fetters](../concepts/ten-fetters.md)

### 22.p684 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-22-p684"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Floods (ogha) are so called because they drag one into the ocean of existence and are hard to cross; yokes (yoga) because they give no release from the object and from suffering. Hindrances (nīvaraṇa) are the five, beginning with sensual desire, by way of covering, hindering and concealing consciousness. Adherence (parāmāsa) is wrong view, since it goes beyond the true nature of each thing and touches on what is untrue. Clingings (upādāna) are the four (sensual pleasures and the rest) as given in the dependent-origination treatment. Latent tendencies (anusaya) are seven, beginning with latent sensual lust, because being strong they lie latent, giving rise repeatedly to lust and the rest. Stains (mala) are three (greed, hate, delusion), being impure themselves and making others impure. Unwholesome courses of action are ten: killing, taking what is not given, misconduct in sensual pleasures, lying, slander, harsh speech, idle talk, covetousness, ill will and wrong view. Unwholesome thoughts are twelve (eight rooted in greed, two in hate, two in delusion). Which knowledge abandons what: the five fetters of personality view, doubt, adherence to rites, and lust and hate that lead to states of loss are to be cut by the first path knowledge; the rest of lust and hate, gross ones, by the second; subtle ones by the third; the five (lust for fine-material existence and the rest) by the fourth. Among defilements, view and doubt are cut by the first, hate by the third; greed, delusion, conceit, sloth, restlessness, shamelessness and fearlessness of wrongdoing by the fourth.

> bhavasāgare ākaḍḍhaṇ'; aṭṭhena duruttaraṇ'; aṭṭhena ca  
oghā ti pi|  
     ārammaṇaviyogassa ceva dukkhaviyogassa ca appadānato  
yogā ti pi tesaṃ yeva adhivacanaṃ|

chādiṭṭhiyā etaṃ adhivacanaṃ|

māna-diṭṭhi-vicikicchā-bhavarāga-avijjānusayo ti evaṃ vuttā  
kāmarāgādayo satta| te hi thāmagatattā punappuna kāma-

concepts: [The ten fetters](../concepts/ten-fetters.md), [The five hindrances (pañca nīvaraṇāni)](../concepts/five-hindrances.md) · obstacles: [The five hindrances (nīvaraṇa)](../obstacles/five-hindrances.md)

### 22.p694 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-22-p694"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Abandoning by substitution of opposites (tad-aṅga-pahāna): abandoning of each state to be abandoned by a factor of knowledge that is its opposite. So defining mentality-materiality abandons personality view; discerning conditions abandons the view of no cause or wrong cause and the stain of doubt; comprehension in groups abandons the grasp of "I" and "mine" as a whole; determining path and not-path abandons the perception of the path in what is not the path; seeing arising abandons the annihilation view; seeing passing abandons the eternity view; appearance as fearful abandons the perception of no danger; seeing danger abandons the perception of enjoyment; contemplation of disenchantment abandons delight; desire for deliverance abandons non-desire for deliverance; reflection abandons non-reflection; equanimity abandons non-equanimity; conformity abandons the taking of the truth contrarily. Or, in the eighteen principal insights: contemplation of impermanence abandons the perception of permanence; of pain, the perception of pleasure; of not-self, the perception of self; of disenchantment, delight; of fading away, lust; of cessation, origin; of relinquishment, grasping; of destruction, the perception of compactness; of passing away, accumulating; of change, the perception of the stable; of the signless, the sign; of the desireless, the wish; of voidness, adherence; of insight into states that is higher understanding, adherence to substance; of knowledge and vision of things as they are, adherence through confusion; of danger, adherence to attachment; of reflection, non-reflection; of turning away, adherence through binding. Destruction contemplation, done by analysing away compactness, sees that impermanent and knows "destruction"; by it the perception of compactness is abandoned. Passing-away contemplation (vayānupassanā) is the resolve on cessation, seeing the breaking up of formations directly and by inference; by it accumulation is abandoned.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind_

concepts: [The kinds of abandoning (pahāna)](../concepts/five-kinds-of-abandoning.md)

### 23 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-23"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

The attainment of cessation is the non-occurrence of consciousness and its concomitants by their progressive cessation; worldlings, stream-enterers, once-returners and dry-insight non-returners and arahants do not attain it; non-returners and arahants who possess the eight attainments do, by possessing the two powers (calm and insight), by tranquillizing the three formations, and by sixteen kinds of knowledge-behaviour and nine of concentration-behaviour.

> kā nirodhasamāpattī ti yā anupubbanirodhavasena cittacetasikānaṃ dhammānaṃ appavatti. … sukkhavipassakā ca anāgāmino arahanto na samāpajjanti. aṭṭha samāpattilābhino pana anāgāmino khīṇāsavā ca samāpajjanti

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: realized · types: powers-experiences, karma-liberation_

terms: [nirodha-samāpatti](../terms/nirodha-samapatti.md) · concepts: [The attainment of cessation](../concepts/nirodha-samapatti.md) · practices: [The attainment of cessation (nirodha-samāpatti)](../practices/nirodha-samapatti.md)

### 23 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-23-2"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Before entering cessation the meditator performs preliminary duties — resolving that the requisites near him not be damaged, attending to the Saṅgha's possible need and the Teacher's summons, and reviewing the limit of his life-span; things not resolved upon can be destroyed by fire, as in the story of the elder Mahānāga.

> anadhiṭṭhahato pana aggi ādīhi vinassati, mahānāgattherassa viya.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: realized · types: powers-experiences, death-dying_

practices: [The attainment of cessation (nirodha-samāpatti)](../practices/nirodha-samapatti.md)

### 23 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-23-3"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence moderate

The benefits of developing understanding are the removal of the various defilements, the experience of the taste of the noble fruit, the ability to attain cessation, and the worthiness to receive offerings.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: knowledge · stage: realized · types: karma-liberation_

concepts: [The attainment of cessation](../concepts/nirodha-samapatti.md), [The four paths and four fruits](../concepts/four-paths-and-fruits.md) · practices: [Attainment of fruition (phala-samāpatti)](../practices/phala-samapatti.md)

### 23 <a id="tea-visuddhimagga-23-4"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

The epilogue says the work was composed at the request of the venerable Saṅghapāla, of the lineage of the Mahāvihāra dwellers, the Theriyas who teach analysis (vibhajjavāda).

> vibhajjavādi-seṭṭhānaṃ theriyānaṃ yasassinaṃ, mahāvihāravāsīnaṃ vaṃsajassa vibhāvino: bhadantasaṅghapālassa

_level: unmarked · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: all · types: teacher-transmission_
